## BL-809 — a re-review pass is spent on evidence and bookkeeping findings that a validator could settle, so a story is reviewed up to nine times

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Graph s319 ran 44 gate-1 re-review passes over 23 reviewed stories. s317 ran 13 over 21
and s318 ran 16 over 14. Story 3.1 ran 9 passes: p5, p8 and p9 carried 0 findings, and p4, p6 and p7's findings
are capture labels, `$` lines, stale citations and story-record wording.
- Finding mix: about 2 in 3 of the 250 `#### ` findings in s319's `-p<M>` files are evidence or bookkeeping
  (167 matched an evidence or bookkeeping keyword set). That is a heading-keyword classification and a floor of
  unknown precision. The derived form is a finding under a part whose map row is entirely captures.
- Code-shaped findings: the remainder include real code findings (a PUT of `inf` returning 500, a live smoke that
  passes silently on null), so the round itself is not the defect.
- BL-510 is PARTIAL: it reaches about 7 of 12 evidence verdicts and 0 of 10 bookkeeping verdicts by its own
  measurement, and the rest still return as review findings.

Owed:
1. A NEEDS_REWORK whose every Critical and Important finding is evidence or bookkeeping re-checks through
   `validate-handoff-evidence.sh` and the one serial reviewer it names, not through a fresh fan-out.
2. A re-review whose delta, captures excluded (BL-808), touches no more than one code file dispatches one serial
   reviewer.
3. Extend the validator to the evidence classes s319's findings show it does not refuse: a capture labelling the
   wrong commit, a `$` line that cannot replay, and a stale per-AC citation.

Item 1 is the wall-clock lever and is not optional. A re-review pass is a serial wait (rework, freeze, review)
whatever its width, so a narrower pass removes dollars and a removed pass removes hours. BL-808 makes passes
narrower and removes none. Reference points: gate-1 re-review passes per reviewed story read 0.62 in s317 (13
over 21) and 1.91 in s319 (44 over 23); implementation plus gate-validation[implementation] machine hours per
story (`steps.csv` active plus agent wait, operator wait excluded) read 1.87 and 1.80. The measured sprint reports
both. A re-review pass is earned by a code finding the pass caught; a pass spent only on evidence or bookkeeping a
validator could settle is the waste this entry removes. The implementation spawn and dollar figure is reported
jointly with BL-808.

**Status (this release).** PARTIAL. Items 1 and 2 shipped, and item 3 shipped in part (its still-owed arms are below); `verify: manual` until the measured sprint.

Item 1, the record-only route. Each Critical/Important finding carries one `Class: CODE|TEST|EVIDENCE|BOOKKEEPING` line (owner `code-reviewer.md`; TEST is code-class; a finding admitting any code fix is CODE). `merge-review-shards.sh` refuses a value outside the set and accepts a missing one. `partition-review-diff.sh --route` routes a re-review to `validate-handoff-evidence.sh` plus one serial reviewer only when every prior Critical/Important finding is EVIDENCE or BOOKKEEPING and the delta from the prior `reviewed-sha:` to the frozen sha carries no code path. It sends the pass full on zero findings, a missing or unknown Class, any unrouted finding, an unresolvable or non-ancestor sha, and an empty or unreadable delta. The delta's code class (`partition-review-diff.sh`, THE CODE CLASS) drops a path under a scan root, a `.md`, a `.txt/.log/.out` outside any test directory, or a `.txt/.log/.out` that `--list-captures` names; any code-extension file is code wherever it sits and whether or not the story cites it. Measured on graph with the shipped predicate: 0/3/6 routed passes (s317/s318/s319). M7's 4 for s318 used a `test-results/` path-prefix partition. The shipped class counts `test-results/red_splat_copy.py`, which the s318 2-3 story cites, as code, so s318 2-3-p3 goes full (fail closed). Labels: blind re-label disagreement was s317 13/86 (5 cross E|B) and s318 5/75 (1 crosses). Without the delta guard, s317 routes 3 passes on M7's labels and 1 on the blind labels, and the s317 2-6 miss appears under one labeller only. The guard removes it under both, so the delta guard is the safety property and Class is the saving.

Guard cost, PRICE / VALUE. VALUE is a pass the guard sent full whose re-review raised at least one CODE or TEST finding; PRICE is every other such pass (clean, or evidence and bookkeeping findings only). The guard sent 11 passes full that the class oracle would have routed: PRICE 10, VALUE 1. VALUE is a full re-review pass that caught a CODE or TEST finding the record-only route would have missed; a pass whose only findings are EVIDENCE or BOOKKEEPING is PRICE, because the route would have settled those.
- s317, PRICE 2, VALUE 1. 1-2-p3 and 1-2-p4 are PRICE. 2-6-p4 is VALUE: it caught 2 code findings that the s317 2-6 rework had introduced by deleting an assertion.
- s318, PRICE 3, VALUE 0. 1-1-p3 is PRICE: its one Important finding (the mutation captures are gitignored and not in the commit) is EVIDENCE. 2-3-p2 is PRICE: its two Important findings (`ac4-hunks.txt` and `ac1-ac3-green.txt` record no command or head sha; the close signal claims a RED for a binding that has no capture) are both EVIDENCE. 2-3-p3 is PRICE: APPROVED with no Critical or Important finding (its one Suggestion is the red-splat driver's exit code).
- s319, PRICE 5, VALUE 0. 2-3b-p4, 3-1-p8, 3-2-p5 and 4-3-p5 are PRICE: each raised no Critical or Important finding. 4-4-p3 is PRICE: its one Important finding is EVIDENCE (`ac1-unavailable` is labelled "AC1 d and AC2 b" but its recapture runs only `-t ST319-4.4-01`), and the verdict stayed APPROVED.
- Derived by reading each pass's review file in graph's clone (read-only), with the labels as the s318 blind re-label and the s319 reading of M4b's findings; the class of each finding is the label, and a pass with no Critical/Important finding has none to label.

Item 3, Rule A. Gate 1 re-runs after a QA rework iff the range adds a test file, or changes a code or test file on a line that is not blank, a comment or a docstring; captures and records never trigger it (`partition-review-diff.sh --gate1-rerun`). Saving in s319: 2 of 44 passes (2-3a-p2 and 3-1-p9, both after QA-only reworks). 2-3a-p3 still re-runs, because 2-3a-b added 26 executable lines. The justified post-APPROVED re-runs 1-1-p3 (18 lines) and 4-4-p4 (1 added spec) still re-run. 3-1-p6 was waste.

Item 2, narrowing a mixed re-review to the files its CODE findings name, is shipped. A CODE or TEST finding carries a column-0 `Files: <repo-relative path>[, ...]` line under its `Class:` line (owner `code-reviewer.md`). `merge-review-shards.sh` THE FILES LINE refuses it on any other class, when two appear in one finding, on an empty list, on an entry that is not a bare repo-relative path, and, at the merge and `--check-shard`, on an entry that is neither tracked at the frozen sha nor changed in the range. A missing `Files:` is accepted. `validate-carried-findings.sh --classes` prints the value as a third column. After `--route` answers FULL, `partition-review-diff.sh --narrow` answers `NARROW:` (the delta's code paths the CODE and TEST findings name) and `RESIDUE:` (every other code path of the delta). It asserts that the two hold every delta code path exactly once. It answers `FULL:` on a CODE or TEST finding with no `Files:`, on an empty named set, and on every refusal `--route` answers FULL on, never exit 2. `--map ... --narrow-from <pass M-1 file>` partitions the named set and puts the residue in one last part, `residue`. A narrowed map never has more parts than the un-narrowed map of the same delta and options (a SERIAL one counts as one part); one that would is refused, exit 2, before anything is written, and the lead runs the un-narrowed partition. The manifest records `narrow-from` and the merge re-derives the map with it. A narrowed manifest re-derived without it is refused whenever the re-derived map differs; an identical partition (an empty residue) is accepted. The step text is `implementation.md`'s "Narrowing a mixed re-review" paragraph. The accepted miss is the 1 of 19 mixed-prior passes over s317 to s319 (s319 3-1-p3 raised a CODE finding, a dead nested function, in `rebalancer/tests/test_s319_3_1_merkl_rewards.py`, a file outside the prior CODE findings' set). The operator accepted it: "The one-in-19 miss rate doesn't matter to me. I want the re-reviews narrowed regardless of the measurement." With the residue reviewed as its own part (option A), that shape is reviewed, not missed: the file is in the delta, so it lands in the `residue` part. The 1 of 19, the C/H and N class readings and the "an empty narrowing set flags 7 of 7" control are prose-heuristic figures from the contract adversary's reading of graph's files, not measurements by the shipping reader. No s317 to s319 prior carries a `Files:` line, so the shipping reader would answer FULL on every one of them.

Receipts (evidence for the shipped mechanism; `verify:` stays manual): the three receipt files (route, rulea, narrow) were removed under BL-915 as unreferenced.

Earlier releases, under the Owed numbering above (kept as written):
- Item 2, closed by BL-808: `partition-review-diff.sh --exclude-captures` answers SERIAL for a counted
  (non-capture) delta of at most one file, whatever the threshold. The re-review step text in
  `implementation.md` cites that SERIAL answer as one reviewer, `shard: 1/1 <story-index>`.
- Item 3, shipped in `validate-handoff-evidence.sh` (handoff mode, the in-range captures arm (a) reads):
  - Arm (m) K-UNQUOTED is a refusal, `k-unquoted`: a `$ ` line that passes `-k` an unquoted multi-word

- Item 3, still owed:
  - Shipped: CAPTURE-UNRESOLVED (`validate-handoff-evidence.sh`, a WARN, exit unchanged) already warns on an

**Progress (batch b256).** PARTIAL; `verify: manual` stays.

Item 3's per-AC arm shipped as a WARN: arm (q) AC-CITATION-UNRESOLVED in `validate-handoff-evidence.sh`, handoff mode, exit unchanged. Population owner: the `## Strategy Test IDs` table that `stories-test-strategy.md` defines (`| Strategy id | Case | Kind | Resolves to |`), the grammar `gate-validation.md`'s "21. Test-strategy deliverable presence (sprint-review gate)" resolves at sprint-review; the arm pulls that check's path resolution forward to gate 1, path part only, and leaves node collectability to Check 21. Grammar: rows whose Kind is exactly `test`; the tokens are the leading unbackticked word of the `Resolves to` cell plus every backticked word in it, trimmed of a `::node` and a `:line` suffix, drawn from `[A-Za-z0-9_./-]`, with no `...` (tested before the punctuation strip, so a trailing `...` is not read either) and an extension on the last segment; a token with a leading `/` is read only when it starts with the physical toplevel, by a lexical prefix test, and is judged by its toplevel-relative rest. Not read: a later table in the section whose header is not the strategy header (the header is bound at its separator row), a table under a later `## ` heading, an unbackticked word that is not the leading one, and a double-backtick span. A token inside the capture grammar is left to CAPTURE-UNRESOLVED through the same `cap_tokens` predicate, so no token is reported twice; every other token is normalised by `cap_norm`. A path with a `/` resolves when it is tracked at the frozen sha; a bare basename resolves when any tracked path has that basename; a token whose extension no tracked file carries is not judged as a path; there is no `.gitignore` acquittal. Measured read-only on a `--no-hardlinks` scratch clone of graph's committed refs (graph `main` 6ee289617) with the shipping script, driven by uncommitted scratch scripts: `pop.sh` lists the passes, `run2.sh` ran the shipping script, the base script and the all-Kinds variant on every s317-s318 pass, and `run3.sh` ran the all-Kinds variant on every s319-s322 pass and the shipping script only where the variant hit. That s319-s322 figure is an exact subset, because the two scripts differ only by the `kind != test` skip, which only subtracts. The per-sprint `test`-row token counts in the arm header are the lines the shipping script's `ACQ_ROWS_AWK` prints per pass, before the capture partition, summed per sprint from `run1.sh`'s per-pass records (the rows re-derive there; the all-Kinds variant's records read other token counts), and they predate the tip awk, which also emits absolute tokens. 212 gate-1 passes over 92 stories, s317-s322, plus 14 UNEVALUABLE (8 reviews with no `reviewed-sha:`, 6 non-story). Over all Kinds the arm would have 11 hits, 1 true positive and 10 false positives: `test` 1 (the TP, s317 2-3 p2 AC6 d, `2-3-ac6-marker-persist-failed.evidence.md`), `predicate` 5 FP (asserted-absent paths, s321 2-1 p1 and p2, 4-2 p1), `unit` 4 FP (a retired test file, s320 3-1 p1 and p2), `live_ops` 1 FP (a deploy-deferred ops artifact, s320 3-3), and `integration`, `e2e`, bash fixture, deploy-time and every other Kind 0. Narrowing: every Kind other than `test` is acquitted, for the reasons the arm header gives (`unit` carries 4 FP, and widening to the zero-hit Kinds would hand-list an open vocabulary). As shipped, `test` only: 1 hit, the TP, and 0 FP. The exit compare against the base script over all 212 passes is the pre-fix run, taken before the reason-column fix and the leading-`../` report, and no exit moved; the post-fix run compared exits on s317-s318 only. The tip-review grammar changes are measured at the final code: the separator-bound header, the elision test before the strip and `-` for an empty cell only subtract, and the TP survives them; the absolute-token rule adds tokens that a scratch clone cannot show, because its toplevel is not the path a story spells, so it is bounded: the shipping `ACQ_ROWS_AWK` at the final code, extracted from the script and run over each of the 212 passes' story at its frozen sha, emits 0 `/`-leading tokens of 1621 `test`-row tokens (0 of 2053 across all Kinds), both totals equal to the earlier records, so 0 under the toplevel, 0 under a worktree path and 0 elsewhere. Controls: in the same run, the token totals equal the earlier records, the toplevel's absolute spelling occurs somewhere in the story text of 188 of the 212 passes (where in the text was not measured) and an impossible token reads 0; in a separate run, a synthetic `test` row citing a nested worktree path is emitted by the same extracted awk. A false-positive path the corpus does not hold was then closed by construction: validation runs from the main checkout and Claude Code nests its worktrees at `<top>/.claude/worktrees/<x>/`, so a tracked file cited by its worktree spelling had an untracked toplevel-relative rest and WARNed. A token under a registered worktree nested in the toplevel is now judged by its worktree-relative rest when that worktree's `HEAD` is the frozen sha, and is not read under any other registered worktree; an unregistered `.claude/worktrees/` path gets no special case. Measured FP set 0 (graph's worktrees are siblings of its toplevel, and `/.claude/worktrees/` occurs in 0 of the 212 story texts), so the false positive is constructible but unobserved, and fixed: the narrowing removes no observed FP and acquits nothing the corpus cites. Cost: at most one lazy `git worktree list --porcelain` per handoff, taken only when an in-toplevel `/`-leading token is present, so 0 new forks on every measured pass. Receipt cells qwt, qwtf, qwtb and qwtun (the pre-worktree validator fails qwt, qwtf and qwtb) and mutants b9-q21 (acquittal removed) and b9-q22 (toplevel-relative rest); the empty Case cell now has cell qemp and mutant b9-q20. The two exclusions the earlier measurement proposed (rows deferred to deploy-validate, and a `git rm` disposition) are not shipped, because neither has a subject among `test` rows. Cost per handoff: one memoised `git ls-tree` of the frozen tree, shared with the ignore check, 0 per-token forks, and the lazy worktree listing above. Measured at the final code: fork-profile TOTAL 2790, equal to base 2790 at the same reading (scripts/fork-profile.sh --section total at the final code), no budget change against FORK_BUDGET 2926 (base 2790 at ee9376900); story-evidence-receipts 36 and story-evidence-receipts-mutants 106, each by two agreeing counted runs, unchanged; 22 b9-q mutants (b9-q1..q22), all killed; the `--matrix` run's 22 of 22 tripped sets equal to the recorded sets.

The refusal-grade form is OWED (lead ruling, batch 256). The WARN ships at 1 TP and 0 FP on a fitted corpus, the 212 passes the arm was narrowed on (11 hits over all Kinds, 1 of them in a `test` row), and a corpus the arm was fitted to cannot show that an ERROR tier is free of false positives. The window is the WARN's own hits over the graph sprints run after a consumer pull carrying this release, every hit adjudicated TP or FP. The WARN is promoted to a refusal only when all three hold: (i) at least 1 adjudicated true positive in the window; (ii) 0 false positives over every pass in the window, with the pass count stated beside the zero (212 today), extended one sprint at a time; (iii) no CONSTRUCTIBLE false positive is known open at promotion time. The worktree token is the example: unobserved in 1621 tokens, and still a reason not to refuse. There is no hit-count floor: at the measured rate, 1 hit in 6 sprints, a floor of 5 is about 30 sprints, a rule that never fires. Promotion is a later release's decision, made by the operator, because a refusal can stop a consumer's sprint.

Outcome NOT MET. Gate-1 re-review passes per reviewed story, from graph `docs/reviews/s<N>/` file names in a scratch clone (graph `main` at 1449b5c72): a re-review pass is a `-p<M>` code-review file with M of 2 or more, and the story key is the file name before `-code-review`. s321 reads 27 over 25 = 1.08 (24 story keys plus the `1.1-ff` fix-forward key; the 9 `sr-*` sprint-review batch keys are excluded; over the 24 story keys alone 27 / 24 = 1.13) and s322 12 over 8 = 1.50, against s317 0.62 and s319 1.91. Control, same read: s317 13 / 21, s318 16 / 14 and s319 44 / 23 reproduce; an impossible sprint `s999` reads absent. s322 is in retro, so its count may not be final. No pass is recorded as routed record-only: `ROUTED` occurs 0 times in the review files of s317 through s322. Machine hours per story were not re-measured on this entry's basis; the data was available. The outcome is owed on the first graph sprint after a consumer pull carrying this release, in passes per story and machine hours per story.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`), cited per BL-817.

## BL-810 — carry-over evaluation runs party, elicitation, cross and adversarial rounds whose yield has never been measured

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Carry-over-evaluation spawns in graph:

| | s317 | s318 | s319 | s320 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Spawns | 49 | 79 | 88 | 114 |
| Dollars | $96 | $110 | $87 | $127 |

s320's step ran 5.8 machine hours, against 2.9 in s318 and 2.8 in s319. Cross shards appeared in s319 (24) and
held in s320 (24); elicitation and adversarial shards grew alongside them.

`docs/analysis/step-value-s317-s319.md` measured requirements, architecture, stories and sprint-review rounds and
never carry-over's. No `Failure caught:` line exists for these rounds. Under the step-value ruling
(`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`, "A step with no measured value goes"), an element with no measured value is
removed or merged by the batch.

Owed: measure s319 and s320 carry-over rounds by the step-value method: REFOUND, MISSED-BY-PRIOR,
REPAIR-INTRODUCED and UNIQUE-SURVIVED, with a high-consequence guard. Remove each round with no unique surviving
catch, and give each kept round its `Failure caught:` line.

**Amended: the measurement goes down to the fan-out width.** A round can earn its place while most of its width
does not, and the round-level measurement cannot see that. For each round, separate the catches the per-part
shards made from those only its cross groups made, with the consequence of each and the dollars and agent-minutes
each width cost. A cross group whose unique surviving catches are none, or are low-consequence catches another
round also makes, is narrowed or merged. One that carries a high-consequence catch no other round makes is kept on
consequence, and states that catch and its cost beside its `Failure caught:` line. Reference points:
carry-over-evaluation read 49 spawns and $96 in s317 (the table above, `origin/main`'s extractor); s320 read 114
and $127 on that extractor, and 132 and $159 on BL-813's corrected one.

**Status (this release).** PARTIAL. The `Failure caught:` lines shipped: each round's line is in
`carry-over-evaluation.md` §3a, pinned by `process-rule-pins` rows `coe-26c-caught` and `coe-26c-loop`. The
round-level measurement in `docs/analysis/step-value-carry-over-s319-s320.md` is recorded as evidence for the
per-round, per-width measurement: party, elicitation and adversarial p1 carry unique surviving catches, and p2's
9 MAJORs sit in text only it could read and all 9 shipped fixed. That evidence does not discharge this entry; the
KEEP or narrow decision per round and per width is c6's, under the step-value ruling.
The amended scope (the measurement per round down to the fan-out width) is not in this release; it rides
cluster c6, a separate release in batch 243. It is not dropped. Receipt for the shipped mechanism (scored text,
not the close predicate): `s=$(LC_ALL=C awk '/^### 3a\. /{g=1;next} g&&/^### /{exit} g' core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/carry-over-evaluation.md); [ -n "$s" ] && grep -qF 'Failure caught:' <<<"$s" && grep -qE '^- Adversarial pass(es)? 2[^:]*: Failure caught:' <<<"$s"`
— it holds the §3a `Failure caught:` lines in place, including adversarial pass 2's. Scored by this rebase: fix 0,
the pre-release `carry-over-evaluation.md` (bd0be5e51) 1. Scores recorded by the hands that built the receipt, under
`set -uo pipefail`: the §3a paragraph deleted 1; the adversarial-loop line missing with the other three
`Failure caught:` lines present 1; the second spelling ("Adversarial pass 2 onward (the convergence loop): Failure
caught:") 0.

## BL-812 — the efficiency program shipped fixes with no per-story outcome target, and the outcome went unmeasured until two sprints later

**DEFECT (efficiency).** The ranked set summed to about $500 of removable cost against roughly $1,700–2,300 per
sprint. Every closed entry closed on a tree receipt, and the "Measuring the change" paragraph under the ranked-set heading
was never run. Per story, subagent dollars were flat ($88, $109, $88) across s317–s319, and machine hours were flat
(4.8, 4.7, 4.7). In the same window, BL-808 through BL-811 added spawns, and BL-510's capture rules fed BL-507's
fan-out instead of reducing it.

Owed:
1. **The outcome is measured on consumer transcripts by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/`
   over the first graph sprint that runs the fixes:** machine hours (lead active plus agent wait, operator wait
   excluded), tokens, subagent dollars and spawns, per story and per step, beside s317 ($88, 4.8 h, 44.5 spawns)
   and s319 ($88, 4.7 h, 78.0). Each step whose cost did not fall carries the step-value evidence for what that
   cost buys. The goal is efficiency, not a number: a fixed bar can be met by cutting a step that catches real
   defects, and the step-value rule decides every cut.
2. **The release that ships the last of BL-808 through BL-811 states that measurement as owed, and this entry
   closes only on the measured sprint,** never on a tree receipt.
3. **The release also states its projected per-story figures for each step it changed,** so that the measured
   sprint is read against a prediction. A step that moved less than projected is explained from the sprint, and
   an entry whose step did not move and carries no value evidence reopens.

**Status (b244 c3; b248).** PARTIAL, `verify: manual`. Items 1 and 2 are owed: they need the measured graph sprint. For
item 3, projections are stated below for the steps 0.948.0, 0.952.0 and 0.989.0 changed. The BL-809 item 2
projection, for 0.989.0 (8f25c6904), is now stated in its own row. Item 3 stays OPEN: BL-809 item 3's WARN on an untracked
per-AC capture citation is shipped (CAPTURE-UNRESOLVED), but its per-AC arm for citations outside the capture grammar
(and any refusal-grade form) is still owed, and the release that ships it changes the gate-1 handoff check and owes its own
projection. Every figure below is a
PROJECTION, a counterfactual on a past sprint, and never a target or a pass bar: the step-value rule decides the
measured sprint, and a projected figure never does. The subject set was derived from `git show --stat ce275f7e9
abd42574d 8f25c6904` and the step, rule-body and role diffs. 0.949.0's step changes (BL-804, BL-807, BL-814, BL-816) are
excluded, because they are not BL-808 through BL-811 and BL-804 is a reopened efficiency entry that owes its own
outcome. Three changes carry no cost of their own and get no row: `gate-validation.md`'s newest-first Recent Activity
wording, its Check 24 arm L text, and the `carry-over-evaluation.md` `Failure caught:` lines. Every s319 per-story
division uses 20 stories and every s320 one uses 13 (`roll.py` STORIES; `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/README.md:81`).
s320's planning figures are provisional, because its stories gate has not passed. API dollars equal token-equivalent
dollars for every s319 and s320 figure, because only s317 ran a local lead (README:65-67). Two instruments are
mixed here. The savings come from each entry's own extraction: BL-808's pass means, its 31.5 spawns and its $30.7
are from the bd0be5e51-era extractor, BL-810's figures are from the BL-813-corrected extractor (0.948.0), and
BL-811's are from its hand's extraction in `docs/analysis/step-value-stories-elicitation-s319-s320.md`. The Basis
dollars and hours are from the scripts as committed at fc71a7b10 on main. So a projected figure, which is baseline minus saving, crosses
the two instruments. The savings on implementation passes overlap: BL-808's flips, BL-809's route, Rule A,
BL-809 item 2's narrowing and BL-811's cross collapse all act on the same gate passes, and the $19.70 sharded mean includes the pass's 6 cross
reviewers. The combined implementation figure is therefore a MEAN-BASED ESTIMATE, the sum of the standalone
mean-based estimates. BL-808's row, BL-809's high end and Rule A's high end are each N passes times the sharded mean
($19.70, 12.9 spawns, 103 agent-minutes). Such an estimate bounds the saving only if the passes that flip cost at or
below the sharded mean, and the tree does not record per-pass cost for the flipped set. An overlap can only lower a
marginal saving, so the sum does not double count. The one genuine ceiling is BL-811's 171 spawns, a count ceiling
and not a mean. BL-808's "19 of 30" sharded s319 passes does not match the 10 + 21 = 31 its own body counts, and the

## BL-819 — the lead session's own hours and tokens are outside every efficiency entry, and they are most of the wall clock

**DEFECT (efficiency).** BL-808 through BL-811 count subagent spawns and subagent dollars, and BL-812 counts
machine hours without saying how much of them is the lead's. No entry attributes hours or dollars to the lead
session, which is the larger share of the machine hours, and whose cost rose. Per story, from this ledger's s317–s320 extraction
(`steps.csv`, pipeline bucket, operator wait excluded, prices as `roll.py`):

| | s317 | s318 | s319 | s320 (through stories) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lead active h / story | 2.93 | 2.48 | 2.74 | 1.29 |
| Agent wait h / story | 1.85 | 2.18 | 1.91 | 1.27 |
| Lead active share of machine hours | 61% | 53% | 59% | 50% |
| Lead $ / story | 15.2 | 23.3 | 23.6 | 10.6 |

The implementation step holds the largest part: lead active 18.7 h in s317 and 17.9 h in s319 [BL-927 extractor fix (b251): now 18.0 h in s317 and 18.1 h in s319; conclusion unchanged] (first given here as 18.7 h in both; the Correction block below has the corrected figure), and lead dollars $41.25
in s317 against $136.54 in s319 (API price; see the Correction block). Gate-validation is second (10.5 h and 9.8 h). Narrowing a parallel fan-out
(BL-808, BL-811) removes subagent tokens and little wall clock, because a fan-out takes as long as its slowest
shard; lead active time is the part of the wall clock that is serial. The lead hours come from the same gap
summation as every machine-hour figure here, which over-counts when timestamps arrive out of order (about +5 h on
s320's total); that changes no direction, and the attribution below should correct it before it builds on it.

Expected movement, a projection to read the measured sprint against and not a bar: implementation lead dollars
per story back toward s317's level at flat or lower lead hours, and lead active hours per story falling in every
step whose lead work is mechanised under item 2.

Owed:
1. **Attribute the lead's active time and tokens inside each step** of s319 and s320, with the extractor
   extended for it: dispatch and brief writing, reading and merging shard output, repair loops, validator and
   gate runs, re-reads of step files, and compaction recovery. Each class gets hours, tokens and dollars per
   story. The implementation step's lead dollars rising from $41.25 to $136.54 is explained from it.
2. **Decide each class by the step-value ruling** ("A step with no measured value goes",
   `.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`, as applied in `docs/analysis/step-value-s317-s319.md`). Lead work that
   carries no catch or decision another element does not already make is removed or mechanised: a merge or a
   check the lead reads and re-derives by hand that a program already answers goes to the program.
3. **Lead hours and lead tokens join every efficiency outcome.** BL-812's measured sprint and BL-817's
   `Outcome measured:` line report lead active hours and lead dollars beside the subagent figures, per step.
   Owed under item 3 (batch b255): **the idle-wait class split.** `roll.py --classes` charges the interval after
   an end-of-turn record to its own class, as `lead_time.py` already does, so that `other` stops reading as lead
   work. Item 3 cannot report lead hours without it.

**Mechanisations (batch 243):**
- **L1, beat polls.** `_gate-procedures.md` "Bounded-join beat" gave no progress-check form; it now prescribes `wait-for-deliverable.sh --complete --status <the wave's paths>`, never a grep, listing or count of the shard directory. s319/s320 had 163+164 and 22+38 hand probes. In a 30-hit sample, 12–14 asked the progress question that per-path `--status` already answers; the rest are partition checks (4), required reads of delivered content (5), orientation (5) and pre-dispatch checks (2). Recorded as already mechanised, with that residual. Pinned by `process-rule-pins` row `gp-beat-status`.
- **L2, gate re-reads.** Already prescribed: `gate-validation.md` and `SKILL.md` direct `gate-checkpoint.sh`. No step text changed. Residual: 4 re-runs in s319 and 0 in s320 — identical validator calls already run before a compaction in the same gate, across the 7 compactions whose recovery block carried the gate-resume section, excluding the prescribed snapshot budget call. All 4 fell in the 2 windows where the lead did not call `gate-checkpoint.sh done`. Recorded as already mechanised.
- **L3, snapshot updates.** Items 1–3 of "Sub-step snapshot update" were one Edit per row; they are now one `bash scripts/ai-dlc/snapshot-update.sh` call. The program runs `validate-artifact-budget.sh --stamps-only` on the lines it adds and writes nothing on a finding. s319: 445 snapshot Edits in 298 consecutive runs; s320: 219 in 123. One call per run is −7.35 and −7.38 calls per story; no dollar figure is claimed. Pinned by `gp-snap-program` and by the program's own fixture `snapshot-update-stamps`. 0.947.0's Deny B had already refused the 451 python and 49 `sed -i` snapshot writes of s319.
- **L3 progress (BL-912).** The snapshot-edit deny (`ai-dlc-snapshot-edit-deny.sh`) is L3's enforcement: a lead Edit or Write of the snapshot is refused and the remedy names the `snapshot-update.sh` op. L3 is judged on BL-912's graph `Outcome measured:` line, and is not closed here.

Reconciliation to the pre-a2 references. The same window run through the extractor before BL-927 (`f83c00016`) reproduces $41.25, 18.73 h and 2,248 calls for s317 implementation, and $136.54, 17.86 h and 2,010 calls for s319. a2 moves gate spans out of `gate[implementation]` and into `implementation`. In s319, implementation rises by $8.31 to $144.85, and `gate[implementation]` falls by the same $8.31 to $67.24. In s317, implementation rises by $16.48 to $57.73, `gate[implementation]` falls by $13.74 and `handoff` falls by $2.73. Lead active hours read 18.01 h for s317 and 18.14 h for s319.

What the class split explains. Of the +$87.12 API rise, $69.58 is the model move: s317's local calls cost $0 at API price, and token-equivalent pricing leaves a real rise of +$17.54 (+13.8%). Calls fell 6%, from 2,199 to 2,069. The real rise sits in four classes: dispatch +$9.78 (73 to 144 calls), gate-run +$4.55 (64 to 103 calls), compaction-recovery +$3.96 (the same 18 calls at more than three times the price) and `other` +$10.53. It is offset by merge −$12.32 (919 to 566 calls), with snapshot roughly flat at +$0.42 (351 to 309 calls).

What the class split cannot explain. Tokens per lead call rose 41%, from 175,491 to 247,618, and cache reads rose from 374.3M to 504.1M. Every class shares that growth, because it is context growth and no class predicate can see it. The `other` rise is mostly end-of-turn text messages and the short waits charged to them, as described above, not a lead activity. The snapshot predicate now includes Bash commands that name `pipeline-snapshot.md`, which moves the implementation snapshot token-equivalent change from −$9.91 to +$0.42 (API from −$1.41 to +$14.12).

**Measured (batch 255 analysis, s321 complete, s322 partial).** `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322.md`. Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code transcripts, 151 lead and 6,018 subagent, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z, extracted by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` with the s321/s322 edge at 2026-11-09T00:00:00Z; s322 is partial (planning complete, 3 of 8 stories done). In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. Two readings. First, the premise is overstated: `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/lead_time.py` splits the extractor's lead "active" column, and the interval after the harness's end-of-turn record, where the lead sits idle until a notification wakes it, is 14.2 h of 56.2 h (s317), 14.5 of 35.1 (s318), 26.3 of 66.6 (s319), 19.8 of 50.3 (s320), 21.7 of 62.2 (s321) and 8.4 of 27.6 (s322): 25 to 41% of "active" is waiting on subagents. On s321 the pipeline bucket's 120.5 h is about 60 h waiting on subagents, 24.9 h operator wait and about 35 h of lead turn time (model plus harness, 8,005 API calls at about 16 s each); the model and harness shares of turn time are not separable from record order. Second, bookkeeping calls: in s321 the lead ran `wait-for-deliverable.sh` 922 times, `snapshot-update.sh` 756 times and `gate-transition.sh` 149 times, 1,827 of 8,005 calls; at 15.9 s and $0.064 a call that is at most 8.1 h and $117 a sprint, an upper bound that assumes one tool call per lead turn. `snapshot-update.sh` ran 802 times in s322 for 8 planned stories, so L3's one call per run is not met although the hand edits stopped (BL-912). Lead snapshot time per story (`roll.py --classes`): 0.26 h in s321, 0.21 h in s322, against 0.25 to 0.35 h in s317 to s320. Filed here as evidence and not as a new entry (lead ai-dlc-7f offered BL-972; it is not used).

**Progress (batch b254, v0.1052.0; the entry stays PARTIAL, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line).** L3's one call per run was not holding because the In-Flight row half did not hold. Measured on graph's s322 lead transcripts (window 2026-11-09T00:00Z to 2026-11-11T10:30Z): 823 `snapshot-update.sh` tool calls ran in 550 runs. The op mix was row-add 305, row-replace 93, row-status 115 and row-delete 173. Of the 442 lead Agent calls, 348 carried no column-0 `Deliverable: <path>` line, counted with the hook's own `deliverable_of` replayed on every prompt. `ai-dlc-inflight-row.sh` raised 260 "in-flight row NOT written" errors, and each one handed the lead a hand-written `--row-add`. The step text already required the line, but the brief ending it tells the lead to copy said "ONE line". Shipped: `ai-dlc-dispatch-guard.sh` refuses a lead dispatch, of a role with a readable team-role file, whose brief yields no deliverable path and no `--mark` mark_file, before the spawn-ledger append. A nested spawn, `run_in_background: false`, no snapshot, a pre-era step file and an `/ai-dlc-update` session are not judged. `deliverable_of` is shared byte-identically with the hook and bound by `dispatch-model-guard`. The brief ending in `_gate-procedures.md` is now TWO lines, `Deliverable: <file>` and the `--mark` line. `snapshot-update.sh` lists every op name on an unknown argument. The s322 residual this does not touch: 98 zero-op `--help`/source-read calls, 18 FUTURE-stamp refusals and 10 guessed op names; only the last is addressed.

Receipt (replay, `verify:` stays manual): the shipping guard, run over the 442 real s322 lead Agent payloads in a scratch consumer world (graph's roles, agents and settings, copied), refuses 265. That includes all 260 dispatches the hook answered "row NOT written". The other 5 never launched in the real run: a Rule 29 pause, three InputValidationErrors and one other guard deny. So 0 launched dispatches are refused. In the same world the origin/main guard refuses none of them, and `cmp` asserts the two replay outputs differ. An independent contract adversary reproduced 260 of 260 with 0 under-denied. The guard treats an absent `run_in_background` key as background, and the corpus bears that out: all 236 absent-key s322 calls that returned a status came back `async_launched`. The fixture battery has 19 cells and 9 mutants, every mutant killed on its deciding cells with its twins held. Projection only, not measured, needs a graph sprint after a pull: −117 `snapshot-update.sh` calls whose only ops were `--row-add`/`--row-replace` for an erroring dispatch, and −10 `--dispatch-ts` lookups. Against that, one re-send per denied batch: 104 assistant turns if the lead never learns, about 56 at one per session or compaction segment (6 sessions, 50 compactions). Net about −23 to −71 lead calls on s322. L3 stays judged on BL-912's graph `Outcome measured:` line (standing dependency, not progress).

Consumer cost: on graph's next pull the guard arrives at step 3b. Its era gate reads the installed `_gate-procedures.md` sentence, which graph has carried since 0.1019.0, so the deny is live from that step, consistent with the installed item 3 that already requires the line. The TWO-line brief ending arrives with the reconcile merge. Until then a lead pays one re-sent dispatch per brief template that lacks the line.

- the s317 and s319 implementation totals in the table above reproduce on this corpus: dispatch $5.64 to $15.41, gate-run $3.46 to $8.01, compaction-recovery $1.71 to $5.67 and other $41.81 to $52.34, at 73/144, 64/103, 18/18 and 756/914 calls. So s317 implementation is intact on the restored corpus.

The dispatch delta reads +$9.77 from the two-decimal components; the table's +$9.78 is the unrounded difference. Hours below are active hours. The `gap` column is excluded throughout because it can hold handoff idle, which is out of scope by ruling. A KEEP removes 0 h and $0 by itself; any s319-to-s321 movement is credited to the change that caused it, never to the decision. Cells are implementation step, lead seat, per story: active h / token-equivalent $ / calls.

| class | s317 | s319 | s321 | decision | removed by this decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| dispatch | 0.026 / 0.27 / 3.5 | 0.062 / 0.77 / 7.2 | 0.030 / 0.38 / 4.6 | KEEP | 0 h, $0 |
| gate-run | 0.018 / 0.16 / 3.0 | 0.023 / 0.40 / 5.2 | 0.027 / 0.40 / 6.1 | KEEP, on consequence | 0 h, $0 |
| compaction-recovery | 0.011 / 0.08 / 0.9 | 0.004 / 0.28 / 0.9 | 0.002 / 0.19 / 0.6 | KEEP | 0 h, $0 |
| other | 0.453 / 1.99 / 36.0 | 0.532 / 2.62 / 45.7 | 0.264 / 1.00 / 17.0 | MERGE | 0 h, $0 as its own step; see below |

- **dispatch: KEEP.**
  - Failure case it prevents: no other element launches a subagent, so every catch the step-value analyses credit to a subagent round passes through it.
  - Its s317-to-s319 rise tracks the fan-out. The s319-to-s321 fall of 0.032 h and $0.39 per story is credited to BL-808 and BL-809, under which implementation spawns per story went from 31.5 to 11.2 (`pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322.md`).
  - The no-value part is the correcting SendMessage sent after a wrong shard directory or a record-format refusal. BL-976 (0.1055.0) and BL-974 (0.1057.0) already guard it, and their saving is a projection, not a measurement.
- **gate-run: KEEP, judged on consequence.**
  - Failure case it prevents: a step advancing over a validator that fails. A gate refusal is the only element that stops that, and no subagent round re-runs the gate's validators.
  - Its per-story cost did not fall from s319 to s321 ($0.40 both; calls 5.2 to 6.1), so value evidence is owed for it (below).
  - The one part the ruling's clause "a check the lead re-derives by hand that a program already answers goes to the program" could reach is a lead calling a validator by hand that `gate-transition.sh` already runs. That redundancy is not established, so it is not recorded as a merge.
- **compaction-recovery: KEEP.**
  - Compaction and resume are normal operation by ruling. No mechanism that refuses, or forces a re-dispatch, because the lead compacted is proposed.

## BL-911 — a planning convergence pass after the first re-dispatches the whole shard map, so every extra pass costs a full-width round of wall clock

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Planning (carry-over-evaluation, requirements, architecture and stories-test-strategy, each with its gate) ran 38.3, 33.1, 35.5, 34.8 and 35.9 machine hours in graph s317 to s321 (s321 open, through stories), whatever the story count. The convergence loop runs in series, and a pass at or after p2 re-dispatches the same width as p1, although `_gate-procedures.md` "Adversarial review dispatch" says pass 2+ "reviews the REPAIR, not the document again".

Adversary spawns per pass, and the span from the pass's first adversary start to its last adversary end:

| Series | p1 | p2 | p3 | p4 | p5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| s321 stories-test-strategy | 36, 179 min | 35, 150 min | 35, 213 min | | |
| s321 architecture | 8, 72 min | 7, 63 min | 7, 72 min | 7, 44 min | 7, 6 min |
| s321 requirements | 13, 63 min | 13, 13 min | 13, 10 min | | |
| s320 stories-test-strategy, p4 to p7 | 19, 16 min | 19, 15 min | 14, 14 min | 14, 8 min | |

Pass-2-and-later adversary plus remediator spawns, dollars and agent-hours: s319 architecture 39, $64, 4.5 h; s320 stories 155, $211, 14.1 h; s321 architecture 52, $105, 11.3 h; s321 stories 96, $95, 10.6 h.

Derivation: `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` and `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s318/scripts/spawns.py`, run on scratch copies with the s320 edge at 2026-10-29T23:20Z and s321 open (token control units==segs 19,866,167,441 on both sides). Then a pass regex `(?:\bp|pass[ _-]?)(\d+)\b` over adversary and remediator spawn descriptions in those steps, with elicitation and party rows excluded. The counts are a FLOOR: 24 of 184 s321 stories rows carry no parseable pass number. s320 stories p1 to p3 span a session handoff and are left out of the table.

BL-508's `siblings:` mechanism (0.786.0) did not cut the pass count. The step-value verdict keeps passes 2 and 3 "while repairs write defects", so the passes stay. This entry removes their width and bounds their number.

Fix, both items required:
1. **A pass at or after p2 reviews only the repair.** Its shard set is the parts, sections or story files that pass M−1's joined repair record edited, plus the one cross agent briefed with the whole subject. It is derived by shipping code from the repair record (`join-remediator-shards.sh` already records each part's `edit:` set), never chosen by the lead. A part the repair did not edit is not re-dispatched. `validate-adversarial-convergence.sh` refuses a pass M≥2 whose shard set is wider than that derived set.
2. **The series is bounded at p3.** A p3 that does not stamp `EXIT_CONDITION_MET` goes to "Divergence resolution dispatch", never to p4. `validate-adversarial-convergence.sh` refuses a p4 or later that does not declare `resolves_divergence:`. This is Rule 8's "divergence is a HARD_BLOCK, not a reason for another pass", given an enforcer.

Fixture arms in the series' own fixture: a seeded p2 whose repair edited 2 of 8 parts dispatches 2 parts plus cross, and the full map is refused. A p4 with no divergence record is refused, and one with it is accepted.

Not discharged by a measurement, a decision record, a prose instruction or a WARN. The entry closes on the shipped refusals plus an `Outcome measured:` line on the first graph sprint that runs them.

Progress: items 1 and 2 shipped, and the entry stays PARTIAL until the `Outcome measured:` line below exists. Item 1 shipped for files (stories) mode: `merge-adversarial-shards.sh --derived-map` prints the shard set a pass after the first owes, derived from notarized bytes (the parts whose bytes changed since the series predecessor, plus the parts the predecessor's CRITICAL and MAJOR findings cite, plus the cross shard, widening to the full map on any unread input); the merge refuses a shard outside it and stamps `shard_set_derived_from:`, and `validate-adversarial-convergence.sh` arm N requires the dispatched set to equal the set recomputed by `--derived-set`. Document and subject modes narrow the same way by part content (operator ruling, batch 256): the merge notarizes a `part_sha:` digest per part and the cut-table runs in `part_run:` (`-` when none) in each pass file, a pass owes the parts whose digest is not uniquely unchanged since its predecessor, the parts its CRITICAL and MAJOR findings cite, and the cross shard, and `--derived-map` prints the full map with a `full width:` reason where a list is missing or unjoinable or the series predates the era token; arm N holds the pass to the printed set. Measured yield beside the mechanism, from a replay of the shipped engine with the era gate forced bound on graph's pass pairs (graph ed61de745): document mode still owes 68 of 72 shards over 11 replayable pairs and subject mode 33 of 42 over 4 pairs, so the saving is small there; the run field added 0 owed shards in every pair (only s321 test-strategy has cut-table runs, and all their members were already owed). Unreplayable: document 3 pairs (s322 carry-over-evaluation p1 to p2, test-strategy p1 to p2 and p2 to p3), subject 1 (s322 requirements p1 to p2, whose p1 file versions are unrecoverable). ## BL-912 — the lead hand-edits `pipeline-snapshot.md` hundreds of times a sprint, and that bookkeeping is the largest single class of lead time

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Lead Edit/Write calls on `_bmad-output/pipeline-snapshot.md`: s320 930 and s321 444. Calls to `snapshot-update.sh`, the sanctioned writer since 0.952.0 (BL-819 L3): s320 2 and s321 0. Of s321's 437 Edits, 242 change In-Flight Teammates rows (agent ids, roles, deliverables, status), 72 change Recent Activity and 6 change table rows.

Lead active time following a snapshot Edit (the gap from that Edit to the lead's next record, gaps of 300 s or less, attributed to the class of the previous lead tool call): s320 planning 4.6 h of 18.2 h (25%), s320 implementation 2.8 h of 15.8 h (17%), s321 planning 4.3 h of 19.1 h (23%). Wait beats are the only class of comparable size (3.2 to 4.9 h), and BL-538 owns them. This attribution is a heuristic over graph's lead transcripts, read only. It was not taken by the shipping extractor.

Fix, both items required:
1. **Hooks write the In-Flight Teammates table, never the lead.** A PostToolUse hook on `Agent`/`Task` appends the row: agent id from the tool result, role, the deliverable from the brief's `--mark` path (the dispatch guard already parses it into `mark_file`), and dispatched-at from `date`. `wait-for-deliverable.sh` sets the status cell when a path reads DELIVERED, MISSING, FAILED, REJECTED or UNLAUNCHED, and the SubagentStop hook sets it when the agent exits. Every write goes through `snapshot-update.sh` `--row-add` and `--row-status`, so the stamp check still runs.
2. **A PreToolUse hook denies Edit and Write of `pipeline-snapshot.md`.** Its remedy text names the `snapshot-update.sh` op for the change. Before it ships, the deny is driven over graph's s319 to s321 lead snapshot Edits. Each Edit no op can express gets a new op in `snapshot-update.sh`. None is exempted from the deny.

Fixture arms: an `Agent` dispatch writes its row with no lead call. A `--mark` delivery flips its status. A lead Edit of the snapshot is denied and names the op. A handoff finalisation is expressible as ops.

**Progress (PARTIAL).** 912a ships item 1 and the ops 912b needs.
- The row hook `ai-dlc-inflight-row.sh` writes the In-Flight Teammates row on an `Agent`/`Task` launch. Its stamp is the spawn ledger's `ts` (not `date`, as item 1 first said), its deliverable comes from the brief's `Deliverable:` line and then `mark_file`, and it is era-gated on `_gate-procedures.md`. SubagentStop and the waiter's `--dispatch`-armed DELIVERED beat flip the row to `delivered-reachable`.
- `snapshot-update.sh` gains the lock and section-scoped key, block, line, row, history and `--init` ops. Its status set is bound to I110.
- Fixtures: `inflight-row-hook`, and the new arms of `snapshot-update-stamps`. That fixture's LIVE arm expresses every NONE class of the s319-s321 walk as ops on a scrubbed copy of graph's live snapshot: row rewrite and replace, placeholder retire, Recent Activity rewrite and trim, key add and delete, Open Items rewrite, Locked Decisions append, handoff finalisation and the initial write.

912b still owes item 2: the PreToolUse deny of a lead Edit/Write of `pipeline-snapshot.md`, and the Deny B rewrite whose remedy names the op. It is gated on every NONE class being expressible as an op, which that LIVE arm now shows on the live shape.

**Progress (deny half).** Item 2 is built. `ai-dlc-snapshot-edit-deny.sh` refuses Edit, Write, MultiEdit and NotebookEdit of `pipeline-snapshot.md` in any case, anchored on no directory, with a remedy naming the `snapshot-update.sh` ops and the FILL sequence. Deny B names ops, skips the sanctioned writer and matches case-insensitively. `snapshot-update.sh` gains `--section-add`, `--section-move-to-history`, `--row-replace`, wrapped-bullet handling and a key-line refusal with a create allowlist. The derivation-capture snapshot branch and `--whole-write` are deleted. The entry stays PARTIAL with `verify: manual` and no LANDED line: it closes only on a graph `Outcome measured:` line. Graph's `route-push.md:67`, `ai-dlc-reset-snapshot.sh:132` and its retro override still direct whole-snapshot edits, so its first sprint start and close will be redirected by the deny.

**s321 baseline, two figures, two methods.**
- 444 Edit/Write: this entry's figure above.
- 435: the s319-s321 walk's figure, from a scan of the 8 lead transcripts that dedupes on `tool_use.id`.

Not discharged by a measurement, a decision record, a prose instruction or a WARN. The entry closes on the shipped hooks plus an `Outcome measured:` line on the first graph sprint that runs them.

## BL-913 — a story's gate transition is a dozen hand-run validator calls by the lead, serially, at every gate pass

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Implementation plus its gate ran 1.80 machine hours per story in s319 and 1.67 in s320. Lead active time was 72% of it, and narrowing review fan-outs (BL-808, BL-811) left that time in place: gate-1 shards per pass fell from 5.5 to 1.9 and subagent dollars per story from $30.7 to $10.8, while machine hours per story fell 7%.

The s320 lead's script calls, all sprint: `verdict.sh` 262, `validate-handoff-evidence.sh` 209, `gate-checkpoint.sh` 185, `partition-review-diff.sh` 101, `validate-gate-adjudication.sh` 77, `validate-artifact-derivations.sh` 70, `validate-adversarial-convergence.sh` 54, `validate-escalation-resolution.sh` 50 and `merge-review-shards.sh` 44. That is over 1,000 calls for 13 stories. Lead active time following a validator or gate-script call (same heuristic as BL-912): s320 implementation 2.9 h of 15.8 h (18%), the largest class there after wait beats. Each call is a lead turn that reads its output and decides the next call, in series, at every gate pass of every story.

A second measurement of the call count: 898 occurrences of the nine scripts in lead turns (`isSidechain` false, Bash `tool_use` commands) across the 7 s320 transcripts in `~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph/` carrying sprint_id 320; impossible-token control 0. The filed 1,052 and this 898 are unreconciled, and both are kept. Of 529 lead calls naming a gate script, 136 would be replaced entirely, 18 are mixed and 375 replace nothing (153 of the `verdict.sh` calls wrap `validate-artifact-budget`). The s320 baseline also predates today's prescription, where `--gate2` and `--smoke` each run once, so the target cannot be read off today's sub-check list.

Fix, required: **one program per gate transition.** `scripts/ai-dlc/gate-transition.sh <story-index> 1|2|3|close [--pass M] -C <worktree> --base <sha> --sprint <N> --story-file <path> [--transcript-dir <dir>]` (shipped from `core/scripts/`; `--transcript-dir` is gate 3 only) runs, in core's order, every check the step files prescribe before dispatching that gate:
- the handoff evidence and smoke arms;
- the capture list;
- `partition-review-diff.sh` `--route`, `--narrow` and `--map`;
- the carried-findings list;
- at gate 3 only, every `adjudication: script` arm of `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/gate-validation.md:169-181`, then opening the checkpoint.

Opening the checkpoint is a gate-3 act and nothing else: `gate-checkpoint.sh open` writes one global OPEN marker (`core/scripts/gate-checkpoint.sh:229`, `:291-293`), so opening it at gate 1 or 2 would overwrite a live gate 3.

After every check has exited 0 it writes ONE dispatch plan file, `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/s<N>/gate-transition/<idx>-g<gate>-p<M>.plan`, carrying the shard list, the route and the brief inputs, and prints its path. A refusal prints the refusals with their remedies on stderr, exits 1, writes no plan and opens nothing. `implementation.md` replaces each per-gate list of validator calls with that one call. The dispatch guard denies a gate-1, gate-2 or gate-3 dispatch whose story has no plan from `gate-transition.sh`. The deny binds the plan to the `.manifest` sha that `partition-review-diff.sh` writes, or at gate 3 to the gate-checkpoint nonce, and never to HEAD. The deny is era-gated on a sentinel line in the installed `implementation.md`.

Fixture arms: a seeded story at gate 1 yields one plan whose shard list equals the one `partition-review-diff.sh --map` gives alone. A story with a stale Dev Agent Record refuses with the handoff refusal. A gate dispatch with no plan at the frozen sha is denied.

Not discharged by a measurement, a decision record, a prose instruction or a WARN. The entry closes on the shipped program and deny plus an `Outcome measured:` line on the first graph sprint that runs them.

**Progress.** The program, the era-gated dispatch-guard deny, the step-file calls and the `gate-transition` fixture shipped in 0.1006.0. The deny covers implementation reviewer and QA dispatches with a shard line and gate-3 adjudicator briefs carrying a `story:` line; a multi-story or storyless gate 3 keeps the lead-run procedure, and `close` falls back to the newest storyless sprint verdict, recording a `gate3-scope:` line. Known gap, ruled "name it, build nothing": a one-story gate-3 adjudicator brief that omits its `story:` line is judged neither by the guard nor by `validate-gate-adjudication`. The entry is PARTIAL until a graph `Outcome measured:` line exists.

**Reading on s322 (batch b256; provisional, not an `Outcome measured:` line).** Window end: graph `ed61de745`, latest lead transcript record read 2026-11-12T10:23:35Z, s322 retro still running, so every whole-sprint figure is OWED.
- s320 baseline: the 898 reproduces exactly on the same 7 transcripts (04c0da6d, 0de9fe4e, ca14f3b7, 517a5b03, 77ef0c7b, 81ea73ea, dd2df1d7), and so do the 529 commands naming a gate script. The 1,052 does not reproduce: the 10 transcripts carrying sprint_id 320 give 1,031 (`verdict.sh` 262, `gate-checkpoint.sh` 185 and `validate-escalation-resolution.sh` 50 equal the filed counts). Of the 154 between 898 and 1,052, 133 sit in 3 earlier transcripts (719a49a0, d71fcbce, d22c7889, all before implementation) and 21 are untraced. The 2.9 h of 15.8 h does not reproduce: the implementation window gives 2.71 h of 13.56 h (20.0%), and two alternative windows give 2.92 h of 17.32 h and 2.96 h of 17.78 h. Control: impossible token `zqxj-never-913` reads 0 in all 14 transcripts (`/usr/bin/grep`), against `verdict.sh` at 5 to 217 lines per file.
- Implementation windows, from the lead's first `steps/implementation.md` reference after planning to its first `steps/sprint-review.md` reference: s320 2026-10-27T06:20:41Z to 2026-10-29T13:25:18Z; s322 2026-11-10T18:47:12Z to 2026-11-12T03:35:48Z.
- Calls in window. s320: 735 occurrences of the nine scripts over 394 commands, 56.5 per story over 13 stories (69.1 over all 7 files; the expected movement's "about 80" is 1,052 / 13). s322: the nine scripts 242 plus `gate-transition.sh` 92, so 334 occurrences over 237 commands (85 name only `gate-transition.sh`). Per script, s320 to s322: `validate-handoff-evidence` 194 to 7, `partition-review-diff` 99 to 2, `verdict` 165 to 31, `gate-checkpoint` 140 to 102, `validate-gate-adjudication` 62 to 24, `validate-escalation-resolution` 24 to 2, `validate-artifact-derivations` 7 to 2, `validate-adversarial-convergence` 3 to 0, `merge-review-shards` 41 to 72 (rose).

## BL-838 — the shipped dev seat is haiku, and implementation reworks rose once dev and QA moved to haiku

**DEFECT (efficiency). Operator ruling 2026-10-27: `dev` → sonnet/medium, `dev-escalated` → sonnet/high.** `templates/settings.json.template` `aiDlcRoles` pins `dev` to haiku/medium and `dev-escalated` to sonnet/medium; 0.781.0 (`6c8ea1d9d`) set both, moving dev from sonnet and dev-escalated from opus/high.

Measured in graph (spawn ledger `model_bound`/`effort_bound`, and story review files by the highest `-p<N>` per story under `docs/reviews/s31N/`):

| Sprint | dev | qa | stories through both gates at pass 1 | gate-1 / gate-2 passes per story |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| s317 | sonnet/medium (82), then haiku/high (40), then haiku/medium (13) | sonnet | 9/17 | 1.71 / 1.18 |
| s318 | haiku/medium | sonnet | 4/13 | 2.23 / 1.38 |
| s319 | haiku/medium | haiku | 4/18 | 3.00 / 1.78 |
| s320 | haiku/medium | haiku | 4/10 | 1.80 / 2.30 |

This is confounded: specs got denser and gates were added over the same sprints, and s317's dev rows carry no story id, so its sonnet and haiku stories cannot be split. The s320 4.2 trace is the direct evidence. Its gate-1 p1 and gate-2 p1 failed on the AC2 literal grep `not.toContain(COMPLETE_HODL.hodl_wallet)`, the AC7 enumeration and the AC7 two-cause state, and gate-1 p4 failed on a control test that never reached the modal. The story file as dispatched (`27481ace9`, 18469 B) states the first three verbatim: the grep 3 times, "two-cause" 2 times. Each miss costs a serial gate pass of about 12 minutes and five seats, so a cheaper dev spawn is not a cheaper story.

QA, consumer side: the template pins `qa` to sonnet/medium, and graph's `.claude/settings.json` overrode it to haiku in the 0.789.0 reconcile (`b3f2fe199`, graph-local). The operator ruled QA back to sonnet/medium. That is graph's setting and is owed in graph, not here. The same reconcile also moved graph's `code-reviewer` from opus to sonnet against the template's opus.

Owed: the template change, `render-agent-definitions.sh` output in the fixtures that pin these tiers re-checked (`core/fixtures/dispatch-model-guard/seed.sh:57-58` seeds its own values), and an `Outcome measured:` line on the next graph sprint, using the table's two columns.

Tree check for the template half, as BL-971's 2026-11-11 ruling leaves the tiers (`dev` sonnet/medium, `dev-escalated` opus/medium; exit 0 on the correct tree, measured 0 on `origin/main` `cb6e0ab80` and 1 on a copy with `dev-escalated` set back to sonnet/high; the predicate as first written, expecting sonnet/high, measured 1 on the correct tree and 0 on that copy): `python3 -c 'import json,sys;r=json.load(open("templates/settings.json.template"))["aiDlcRoles"];sys.exit(0 if (r["dev"]["model"],r["dev"]["effort"],r["dev-escalated"]["model"],r["dev-escalated"]["effort"])==("sonnet","medium","opus","medium") else 1)'`. It is not the `verify:` line because `validate-backlog-receipts.sh`'s seed grammar cannot spell a python predicate (R2 reads it UNSCORABLE), and the entry closes on the outcome line anyway.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

## BL-839 — a story held on a lead precondition falls out of the lead's worklist, and nothing re-surfaces it

**DEFECT. BLOCKER-grade in s320.** The s320 wave plan (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/s320/wave-plan.md`, written 06:25Z) holds 2.1 and then 2.2 "for the amendment": the lead commits 2.1's `## Tolerance (pre-stated)` through a PM re-dispatch before the ops dispatch (story 2.1 AC2, line 115). Sixteen hours later the amendment had not happened: 0 spawn-ledger rows since 02:00 name 2.1 or 2.2 (control: 1.3 has 5), 0 `pm` spawns since 02:00 (control: 42 earlier in the sprint), the story file's last commit is 01:50, and `pipeline-snapshot.md` carries neither story in its worklist. The lead session compacted 38 times over that span. 2.2 waits on 2.1's `## Outcome`, so the chain still has to run in series after every other story is done.

The wave plan is a document the lead wrote once. Nothing reads it back, and no snapshot field carries a held story's unblock action, so after a compaction the hold is invisible.

Owed: every held story and its unblock action carried as a snapshot field that the post-compact digest renders, and a check at the implementation step's exit (and at each wave join) that every `ready-for-dev` story is dispatched or names its hold. Probe it with a seeded story held on an amendment that never lands.

**PARTIAL (batch b245, v0.968.0).** The mechanism ships; the entry closes only on an `Outcome measured:` line from a consumer sprint, under BL-817's regime. What ships:
- The grammar is one Open Items line, `- held: <story-id> until <unblock action> since <epoch>`, written with `snapshot-update.sh --open-item` and removed with `--close-item 'held: <key> '` (the trailing space is required, since it separates 320-1 from 320-10).
- `core/scripts/validate-held-stories.sh` carries four findings: UNACCOUNTED (a `ready-for-dev` story with neither a `held:` line nor an In-Flight row), STALE-HOLD (a hold on a story past `ready-for-dev`), MALFORMED-HOLD (a `- held:` line that does not parse) and AGED-HOLD (a hold older than `AI_DLC_HOLD_MAX_HOURS`, default 4). Exit 0 clean, 1 any finding, 2 unevaluable.
- Dispatch evidence comes from the snapshot's In-Flight Teammates rows, never the spawn ledger, because the s320 ledger rows carry no story token (14 of 14 dev and ops rows), while 33 of 33 s320 snapshot rows name their story. Story ids match across the yaml key, the `<epic>.<index>` form and the file stem, joined through each entry's `file:` field.
- The validator reads stories through the new `sprint-status.sh list-stories` mode (`<key>`, `<status>`, `<file>`, tab-separated) and never parses yaml itself.
- Run sites: the sub-step snapshot update in `_gate-procedures.md` runs all four findings, and `implementation.md` section 7 runs it for STALE-HOLD only.
- At wave-plan time every story not in wave 1 is written as a `held:` line naming its predecessor, and `ai-dlc-recover.sh` names every `held:` Open Item in its verification-turn list.
- Fixture `held-stories` (it ships) has arms h0 (a sprint with no story entries, clean), h1 (the motivating case, exit 0), h2 (UNACCOUNTED), h3 (STALE-HOLD), h4 (malformed), h5 (no snapshot, no Open Items, or no yaml: exit 2), h6 (an In-Flight row's role accounts), h6b (a row naming `story 1.30` does not account 1.3), h6n (a ledger-only near-miss still exits 1), h7 (a 16h hold on an amendment that never lands, which reads AGED-HOLD), h7n (a 1h hold, quiet), h8 (a wave-2 hold naming a wave-1 story), h9 (`held: 2.1` matches yaml key story-320-4 through its file stem), and S1 and S2, binding the `implementation.md` section 7 invocation and the recover hook's `held:` directive. The `_gate-procedures.md` wave-join site and the `_dispatch-protocol.md` wave-plan line are pinned in `process-rule-pins`.
- Mutants, measured by `--matrix`: m-keyonly, m-noage, m-nostale, m-norows and m-fnrnr each die on their deciding arm (h9, h7, h3, h6, h0), and every one but m-norows trips that arm alone.
- Packaging hazard, fixed by BL-892: `scripts/install.sh:669` copied a fixture with a non-recursive `cp <fixture>/*`, so a fixture that shipped a seed SUBDIRECTORY was predicted to lose it on a consumer and pass only here; the copy is now tracked-only, from `git ls-files` (`scripts/install.sh:694-709`). No I74 arm refuses a fixture subdirectory: (b) checks that the loop derives and excludes `.dist-only`, (d) that a marker has a reason, and (e) that a fixture is declared. No tracked fixture carries one today (`git ls-files 'core/fixtures/*/*/*'` returns 0, against 1156 for `core/fixtures/*/*`). `check-31-ac-falsifiability` builds its `--stories-dir` worlds from flat seeds for this reason. BL-892 carries this hazard: it made the copy recursive, and it measured that the flat copy aborted the install (exit 1 under `set -e`) rather than dropping the subdirectory silently.

Spec correction: the owed text says a post-compact digest renders the held field. It cannot. The digest is a byte selection of `SKILL.md` (`scripts/render-postcompact-digest.sh`) and carries no per-sprint state. The carrier is the snapshot Read that `ai-dlc-recover.sh` mandates after a compaction.

## BL-840 — a story can set a dated read window that becomes the sprint's earliest close, with no deferral path stated

**DEFECT.** s320 story 1.3 AC4 scores the s319 monitor "operator press by T+7", ceiling 2026-10-29T21:36:22Z. Its dependencies line requires that AC4 "runs in the final 12 hours before its ceiling" (from epoch 1793266582, 05:36 EDT on 10-29). So the sprint cannot close before then, two days after every other story could have, and nothing in readiness, stories adversarial or the wave plan surfaced that. The closing writer's `deferred_acs` path (`implementation.md:167`) already exists, and s320 used it for 1.2 AC4, 3.3 AC4/AC5 and 3.4 AC7/AC8, but the story does not name it for AC4.

Owed: the stories step (or readiness) refuses an AC whose earliest permitted run time falls after the sprint's planned close unless the AC states its deferral (`deferred_acs` and the carry-over item). The wave plan states the sprint's earliest close derived from those windows.

**PARTIAL (batch b245, v0.968.0).** The refusal ships; the entry closes only on an `Outcome measured:` line from a consumer sprint, under BL-817's regime. What ships:
- Per-AC story lines `run_not_before: <ISO-8601 UTC, Z>` and `defers_to: CO-S<N>-<descriptor>`, in the shape of `falsifiability_waiver:`.
- A sprint-level `planned_close: <ISO-8601 UTC, Z>` key in the sprint-status yaml, in `core/schemas/sprint-status.json`. Its producer is the lead at Sprint Planning, section 3 of `stories-test-strategy.md`.
- The refusal `run-window-after-close <story> <AC>` for a window later than `planned_close` with no `defers_to`, and a refusal naming a malformed timestamp.
- The floor is Check 28's (`validate-spec-adoption.sh --verdict`), asked about the sprint-status yaml's own `sprint:`, because no mechanical creation-sprint source exists. Below the floor the check prints `SKIP RUN-WINDOW (below floor)` and leaves the exit unchanged; an undeclared or malformed floor also SKIPs, since Check 28 already fails those states. At or above it, `planned-close-missing` is refused only when a window exists.
- A whole-dir run (`--stories-dir`) prints one `EARLIEST-CLOSE <iso>` line, the max over non-deferred windows, and the wave plan copies that rendered value.
- Fixture `check-31-ac-falsifiability` gains arms w1 (the s320 1.3 AC4 shape, refused), w2 (the same with `defers_to`, accepted), w3, w4, w4b, w4n, w5, w5d, w6 and w6n. Its mutants defer-ignored, cmp-inverted, floor-ignored and earliest-counts-deferred each die on exactly their deciding arms (w2; w1 and w3; w4; w6), measured by `--matrix`.

Residual, shipped as join J1 (batch b248). `sprint-status.sh check-stories` prints a `REPORT [<key>]` when a story is `done`, its `deferred_acs` parses, and the story file carries an AC with `defers_to:` whose id `deferred_acs` does not list. The AC id is compared exactly and case-sensitively, never the CO value. A missing `deferred_acs` is left to the existing writer_forgot REPORT. J1 is a REPORT in writer_forgot's tier and never moves the exit code. The story's ACs come from one grammar: `validate-ac-falsifiability.sh --list-defers <story-file>...`, which prints `<story>\t<AC-id>\t<CO-id>` per AC carrying `defers_to`, and `sprint-status.sh` calls that mode as its sibling. J2 (a `deferred_acs` id whose AC has no `defers_to`) is ruled out: all 44 graph fires (s319 and s320 closes) were legitimate gate-3 deferrals per `deploy-validate.md:427-441`, so it would flag correct closes. The fixture cells exist: J1 and its near-misses in `core/fixtures/sprint-status-lifecycle/run.sh:661-695` (a_J1, a_J1N, a_J1X, a_J1ABS), and `--list-defers` in `core/fixtures/check-31-ac-falsifiability/run.sh:431-443` (cell ld). BL-840 stays PARTIAL until the `Outcome measured:` line.

## BL-841 — while an AskUserQuestion is pending, the lead advances no lane

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Today's nine AskUserQuestion waits add up to 242 minutes (`tool_use` to `tool_result`, lead transcripts). The lead is single-threaded on the question, so every lane whose next step is ready waits behind it. Clearest case: the question at 02:57 waited 95 minutes; story 1.1's gate-2 PASS committed at 03:11, and the next spawn-ledger row is at 04:35 (0 rows between 03:11 and 04:34). The spawn ledger's long gaps on 10-27 (101, 56, 44 and 69 minutes) line up with the 02:57, 05:22, 06:22 and 07:06 questions. Of the nine, two were mechanical Check 22 questions (BL-842), and one (4.1 AC13, unmeetable as written) genuinely gated its story.

Owed: before a blocking question, the lead dispatches every ready action on lanes the question does not touch and arms their joins; the question names the lanes it holds. Measure it as lane idle minutes during operator waits on the next graph sprint. Handoff gaps stay excluded by ruling; an operator wait inside a session is not a handoff gap.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

**PARTIAL (v0.968.0).** `core/skills/ai-dlc/SKILL.md` "Before any question reaches the operator" now has four items; the fourth is to dispatch every ready lane, arm its join and name the held lanes in the question. The sentence is pinned in `process-rule-pins`. No behavioural replay is built, by standing ruling. Still owed: the `Outcome measured:` line (lane idle minutes during operator waits), taken on a later graph sprint under the BL-817 close rule.

**Measured null (batch 250, close).** Graph sprint 321 was read at e32f9ce2f (read only), with the efficiency scripts taken from extractor sha a797fd022 (0.1002.0, whose scripts/ tree is byte-equal to the b250-close-extractor commit the figures were first taken at) (s320/s321 edge 2026-10-30T00:10:03Z, STORIES[321]=25), cited as `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/` plus the basename. Sprint 321 is planning only: requirements are complete (gate 10/10 PASS, verdict planning-20261103T035443Z at c3d3c0a3f), architecture is complete (planning-20261104T044138Z at 00621e45d), stories-test-strategy is partial, carry-over-evaluation did not run, and there is no implementation yet. Every s321 step is outcome-era, because the fix text is present at graph's s321 fork base 0672c7221.

Claim 1, lane-idle minutes, is UNMEASURABLE (not taken, sprint incomplete). The definition is pending-ask minutes with no subagent transcript active, using `extract.py`'s 300 s active rule. The `operator_asks.py` pairing gives s320 63 calls over 50.9 h and s321 24 calls over 4.0 h; the control, the 10-27 06:57Z ask, reads 95 min waited and 89 idle. Lane-idle minutes are s320 2957 of 3052 (97%) and s321 216 of 242 (89%), but s321 is planning only, so no outcome minutes claim is taken.

Claim 2, that the question names the lanes it holds, is a MEASURED NULL on a shipped fix: item (4) was installed throughout (graph `SKILL.md:361-362` at 0672c7221), and 0 of 24 s321 asks name a lane. The controls came back non-zero: 5 of 63 s320 asks carry the word, and 151 s321 lead text blocks do. A broader grammar found 5 of 24 s321 asks, and none of those names a held lane. Item (4)'s prose did not change behaviour on s321. This is a null for the step's rule, not a pass, and no new entry is filed. Lead-seat local-model share of calls is s320 0% and s321 14% (465 of 3334 calls); subagent seats ran hosted models only (s321 sonnet 14089 calls, opus 1414 calls). These seat call counts were taken from the transcripts through `units.jsonl`, not from graph's spawn ledger. The entry stays live and `verify: manual` stays.

**Measured (batch 255 analysis, s321 complete, s322 partial).** `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322.md`. Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code transcripts, 151 lead and 6,018 subagent, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z, extracted by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` with the s321/s322 edge at 2026-11-09T00:00:00Z; s322 is partial (planning complete, 3 of 8 stories done). In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. Operator wait in the pipeline bucket (`roll.py`, gaps over 300 s with an `AskUserQuestion` open): s320 47.9 h of 117.8 h, s321 24.9 h of 120.5 h, s322 8.8 h of 56.6 h. `operator_asks.py`: s321 57 calls, 100 questions, 26.3 h of answer wait, 6 waits over an hour; s322 17 calls, 8.9 h, 2 over an hour. Dollars: $0, a wait bills nothing. Lane-idle minutes were not re-taken on the complete s321, so claim 1 stays owed. The claim-2 null above stands on the complete sprint's ask count as well: the prose changed nothing that these figures can see. Fix direction, not built: a mechanism in place of item (4)'s prose, so that a question cannot go out while a lane it does not name has a ready action undispatched. Filed here as evidence and not as a new entry (lead ai-dlc-7f offered BL-971; it is not used).

**PARTIAL (v0.1053.0) — the entry's fix direction measured against its own data; the mechanism that could move the number is filed as BL-972.** `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/ask_split.py` (output `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/out/ask_split.txt`, run with `EXTRACT_WIN_END=2026-11-11T10:30:00Z` per that analysis's Reproduce block) sorts each lead `AskUserQuestion` wait into one of three buckets, by what the same lead session's subagents did during it: FINISHED-INSIDE (a subagent started before the ask wrote its last record during the wait), NONE-RUNNING, or RUNNING-NO-FINISH. Population: graph's lead transcripts and their subagent transcripts, read only, 247 asks over s317–s322, with sprints from `extract.py`'s `EDGES`. For s317–s321, its per-sprint call counts and hours equal `operator_asks.py`'s; s322 differs only because one ask falls after the window end. Seven controls each exit 1. One is a self-probe on a seeded root that runs before the corpus. Three are known rows: this entry's 10-27 06:57Z ask is FINISHED-INSIDE (95 min, 2 subagents finished inside it), the 11-08 04:34Z 645-minute "Router PUT" ask is NONE-RUNNING, and the 11-08 04:02Z ask, with one finisher, is FINISHED-INSIDE. The remaining three cover the asks active only through the 300 s tail, an agent notification inside every FINISHED-INSIDE ask, and a notification counter that saw at least one enqueue. Nine mutants of the bucket rules and counters were run at build against the real corpus; each exits 1. On s321 (1576 min over 57 asks): NONE-RUNNING 1086 min (69%), RUNNING-NO-FINISH 325 (21%), FINISHED-INSIDE 164 (10%). On s320: 372, 1889 and 791 of 3052. The "Owed" direction is to dispatch every ready lane before the question. It acts only where a ready action was undispatched at the ask, and the transcripts cannot say where that was, in any bucket. Most of s321's NONE-RUNNING minutes, 940 of 1086, are deploy-validate asks with nothing running, operator actions on the serial deploy path ("Router PUT" 645 min, "Merge #1305" 264 min; the headers are in the output's last column). FINISHED-INSIDE is the one bucket whose cost is visible in the record. There, pre-ask dispatch had already happened: the work was dispatched and finished while the ask held the lead. Every one of those asks has an agent notification enqueued inside it, and none reached the lead before the answer. That is BL-972. No hook ships here. Lead ai-dlc-7f ruled out an `updatedInput` line naming the held lanes on the question (claim 2 made mechanical), because it moves zero minutes; the operator may revisit that. Receipt for the instrument: re-run it per the Reproduce block. The run must exit 0 with every `CONTROL` line OK, and its s317–s321 per-sprint totals must equal `operator_asks.py`'s. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: totals that agree while the buckets are wrong; the controls and the self-probe exist to refuse that. What this does not establish: whether a ready action existed undispatched at any ask. Subagents dispatched from a lead session other than the ask's are not joined. Nor is this claim 1: lane-idle minutes during a pending ask are a different quantity, the script does not compute them, and claim 1 stays owed. The claim-2 null stands.

**Correction (2026-11-11, b255 F4b).** `ask_split.py` now deduplicates asks by `tool_use` id, and one s321 ask (`toolu_011BG6yiWiriWBnJRhiK2p48`, 23.9 min) that sits in two lead files is counted once. Under the deduplicated script s321 reads 56 asks, 1552 min, NONE-RUNNING 1063 and deploy-validate 916, against the 57, 1576, 1086 and 940 above. `operator_asks.py` still counts 57, so the totals equality holds for s317-s320 and differs on s321 by exactly that one duplicated row.
