## BL-972 — a pending AskUserQuestion holds the lead, so a lane that finishes during the question waits for the answer

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code lead transcripts and their subagent transcripts, read only, to 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z: 247 asks over s317–s322, with sprints from the `EDGES` in `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py`. s322 is partial. In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. Measured by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/ask_split.py` (output `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/out/ask_split.txt`). Its per-sprint call counts and hours equal `operator_asks.py`'s for s317–s321. A wait is FINISHED-INSIDE when a subagent of the same lead session, started before the ask, wrote its last record during it. FINISHED-INSIDE minutes of all answer-wait minutes: s317 197 of 398, s318 271 of 811, s319 164 of 1113, s320 791 of 3052 (26%), s321 164 of 1576 (10%), s322 20 of 537. Those lanes are not joined or followed while the question is open. 290 task notifications were enqueued inside 77 of the 247 asks: 133 from agents, the rest from background Bash and Monitor. Every FINISHED-INSIDE ask has at least one agent notification. Yet no notification-carrying attachment or user record, and no lead assistant record, falls between any question and its answer (`DLV-INSIDE` 0, `ACT-INSIDE` 0). The script's self-probe proves both counters fire on a seeded inside record and stay at 0 on a same-message near-miss. 67 queue `remove` records do fall inside, and no record shows the lead's model receiving those before the answer. Control, asserted in the script: BL-841's 10-27 06:57Z ask scores FINISHED-INSIDE (95 min; two subagents finished inside it). Dollars: $0, a wait bills nothing.

The ceiling, stated so the entry is not read as owning the whole 26.3 h. The figure that names this defect is FINISHED-INSIDE. Most of s321's largest bucket, NONE-RUNNING, 1086 of 1576 minutes (69%), is deploy-validate asks with nothing running: 940 minutes, including the 645-minute "Router PUT" and the 264-minute "Merge #1305". These are operator actions on the serial deploy path, and no lead-side mechanism shortens an operator's answer. FINISHED-INSIDE also UNDERCOUNTS, in two ways. An agent that finished inside a wait and was resumed later has its last record after the ask: 3 asks outside FINISHED-INSIDE (397 min, all sprints) carry an enqueued agent `finished` notification. And background Bash and Monitor completions held by an ask are not bucketed at all. A lead that keeps working while a question is open could also dispatch ready lanes inside a wait of any bucket, and no transcript says where those were.

**Correction (2026-11-11, b255 F4b).** `ask_split.py` now deduplicates asks by `tool_use` id, so one s321 ask (23.9 min) that sits in two lead files is counted once. Under the deduplicated script the population above reads 246 asks, and s321 reads 1552 min with NONE-RUNNING 1063 and deploy-validate 916, against 247, 1576, 1086 and 940. FINISHED-INSIDE 164 is unchanged in minutes, and its share reads 164 of 1552 (11%) where the s321 figure above says 164 of 1576 (10%); NONE-RUNNING reads 1063 of 1552 (68%) where the ceiling paragraph says 1086 of 1576 (69%); any reading of s321's FINISHED-INSIDE minutes is therefore against 164 of 1552. The receipt's s321 ask-count baseline is `ask_split.py`'s deduplicated 56, not `operator_asks.py`'s 57. The two tools differ by that one duplicated row, so a closer must compare the ask count only within `ask_split.py`'s output, never across the two tools, and a count of 56 against 57 is not a fall in asks.

Fix direction, built at batch 256 (the Progress paragraph below). The contact shape is ruled. Immediately before the question, the lead joins every finished lane and dispatches every lane the question does not hold. The question itself goes out at once, in `AskUserQuestion`, unchanged. Asking in prose instead of the tool is NOT this fix: Rule 11(a) requires `AskUserQuestion` for an ambiguity question, and `core/hooks/ai-dlc-continue.sh:1637` records the measured failure of the prose form (the operator: "you haven't asked any question ... buried in the transcript"). No variant may suppress, batch away, defer or hold back a question, or lower the ask count. Whether the harness offers a non-blocking ask is unmeasured; BL-994 owns that probe.

**Progress (batch 256).** Operator ruling, batch 256, on contact shape: option 1, "dispatch, then ask". Immediately before each `AskUserQuestion`, the lead joins every finished lane and dispatches every lane the question does not hold, then asks at once in `AskUserQuestion`, unchanged. Option 2 (a non-blocking ask) and option 3 (accept the hold) were not chosen; option 2's harness question is filed as BL-994. Landed: `core/skills/ai-dlc/SKILL.md` gains the subsection `#### Operator decisions: dispatch, then ask (Rule 11(a))`, and Rule 11(a) item (4) now cites it. `core/fixtures/process-rule-pins/run.sh` pins that subsection's paragraph with the `exact`-mode row `r11a-dispatch-ask`, and its deferral mutants `r11a-dispatch-ask-D1` (the paragraph replaced by "Before every `AskUserQuestion`, wait until every running lane has finished, join them, then ask.") and `r11a-dispatch-ask-D2` (the drain sentence "Ask once every running lane has finished." appended) are both killed. The pin's `exact` row also requires its subsection to hold exactly one paragraph, so a drain paragraph added beside it fails the pin too. `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/ask_split.py` gains the HELD-AT-ASK, DISPATCHED-AT-ASK and ADJ-DISPATCH columns that the Receipt below reads. Two contract-adversary findings, which are the adversary's figures and are not re-derived here. First, item (4) of Rule 11(a) ("Dispatch ready actions on free lanes ...") was already live in graph's installed engine through s321, which ran 0.969.0 to 0.1028.0. Second, at all 10 of s321's FINISHED-INSIDE asks nothing was visibly held: the lanes finished after the question went out, so a join before the ask cannot reach them. ACT-INSIDE is dropped as the close key. It counts lead records positioned while the ask's tool_result is pending (`ask_split.py`'s ACT counter), so under a blocking ask a compliant lead reads 0 by construction, and DLV-INSIDE is 0 on every baseline ask. The literal first-draft HELD and DISPATCHED definitions failed too. Tail-only lanes scored as dispatched; DISPATCHED now opens a lane only at a harness-confirmed dispatch or resume and closes it at the agent's terminal notice, with no subagent record timestamp and no 300 s tail. Race notices scored as held; HELD now counts only an agent notice enqueued before the `api-request` record that began the ask's response, and one enqueued after it is a race and is not held. Known limits of the pin: inside its subsection the `exact` row does not catch a drain instruction written as a `#####` sub-heading, because headings are skipped; one inside a fenced block; or one inside an HTML comment, because fences and comments are invisible to every test. The lead model reads raw markdown, so these are real survivors; closing them changes the fixture's grammar for every pin, which is a separate change.

Baseline: s321
Step: gate-validation[implementation]
Expected movement: by operator ruling, batch 256, ADJ-DISPATCH (`ask_split.py`, the per-sprint "ADJ-DISPATCH n of N" figure) rises above 9.5%, s320's 6 of 63 asks, the highest pre-rule sprint measured (`docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/out/ask_split.txt`). Corrected HELD-AT-ASK stays 0 on the closing sprint. The ask count does not fall against s321's 56.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`). The step judged here is the lead's idle hold, never an ask: every question still goes out. Related: BL-841.

Receipt: `verify: manual`, by operator ruling, batch 256. BL-972 closes on an `Outcome measured:` line from a graph sprint read by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/ask_split.py`, giving that sprint's ADJ-DISPATCH rate above 9.5%. ADJ-DISPATCH counts an ask whose own message, or the lead message just before it, carries an `Agent` dispatch with `toolUseResult.isAsync` true. The bar is s320's 6 of 63 asks (9.5%), the highest pre-rule sprint measured, set by a second operator ruling, batch 256. The pre-rule spread, from `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/out/ask_split.txt`, is s317 0 of 13, s318 0 of 26, s319 4 of 71 (5.6%), s320 6 of 63 (9.5%), s321 4 of 56 (7.1%) and s322 0 of 17. The first bar, above s321's 7.1%, was replaced because it sat inside that spread. Its population is graph's Claude Code lead transcripts and their subagent transcripts under `~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph/`, read only, with sprints bounded by `EDGES` in `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` and the window ending at `EXTRACT_WIN_END=2026-11-11T10:30:00Z`. The baseline re-run was on an earlier version of `ask_split.py` in this release, before the `defer_plus_bg` arm and the DRAINED-AT-ASK column were added: a builder hand under the b256-N10 owner re-ran it at that window on a fresh `extract.py` output, its output was byte-identical to the committed `out/ask_split.txt` (`cmp` exit 0), and the owner confirmed the cmp. That run supersedes the contract adversary's earlier estimate of "about 2 of 56". At `ask_split.py` blob f9e83d26dfb6 (`out/ask_split.txt` blob cf6978c4ad1c, this release) the ADJ, HELD and DISPATCHED values are unchanged: columns 1-20 of every ask row and every per-sprint ADJ/HELD/DISPATCHED line are byte-identical to the output of an earlier version of `ask_split.py` in this release, before the `defer_plus_bg` arm and the DRAINED-AT-ASK column were added, and the only changes are added lines and the added `drained_at_ask` column. Corrected HELD-AT-ASK is a regression guard, and it must read 0 on the closing sprint itself; s321 reads 0 asks above 0. Two older sprints carry a non-zero reading, each of 1, in the same file: s319 2026-10-23T00:20:46Z, which is api-request anchored, and s318 2026-10-12T03:13:16Z, which is fallback-anchored. Fallback-anchored means the ask has no `api-request` record, as in s317/s318-era transcripts, so the visibility boundary falls back to the last `user` record before the ask's message. That anchor agrees with the api anchor on all 207 api-anchored asks (FB-DISAGREE 0, with 39 asks on the fallback). The ask count must not fall. A fall against s321's 56 is a contact regression: it is never credited, and it blocks the close whatever else moved. The count is compared only within `ask_split.py`'s output, never against `operator_asks.py`'s 57 (the Correction above). DISPATCHED-AT-ASK (s321 16 of 56, 28.6%, same file) is reported beside the close and is not a close condition. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a lead that holds a question back until its lanes drain. That turns every FINISHED-INSIDE wait into NONE-RUNNING and moves the minutes to 0 with the ask count intact. It is the deferral this entry forbids. The pure deferral dispatches nothing adjacent to the ask and reads ADJ 0, which is why the close is keyed on ADJ-DISPATCH and not on minutes. ADJ-DISPATCH alone cannot tell a lead that drains its lanes and then dispatches (it reads ADJ 1, HELD 0) from a compliant one (lead ruling, batch 256; `ask_split.py` probe (q) records it as the KNOWN LIMIT `defer_plus_bg`). DRAINED-AT-ASK is the column a reader checks for that shape. It is reported, never a close guard. DRAINED-AT-ASK, from the same file: s317 2 of 13, s318 2 of 26, s319 4 of 71, s320 4 of 63, s321 4 of 56 and s322 0 of 17. ADJ-DISPATCH and DRAINED-AT-ASK together: 1 of 56 in s321 (2026-11-08T16:24:12Z, the drain-then-dispatch shape in real data) and 0 in every other sprint, s320 included. ACT-INSIDE is no longer the key, because a compliant lead reads 0 on it by construction under a blocking ask. Fewer or faster-answered questions shrink every bucket without the mechanism, which the ask-count rule refuses. What this does not establish: whether every ready action was dispatched cannot be measured from a transcript, because no record names the actions that were ready. ADJ-DISPATCH shows a background dispatch next to the ask, not that it was the whole ready set. Subagents dispatched from another lead session are not joined, and asks issued from a subagent are not counted. Every column here is positional against a blocking ask. If the harness's ask mechanism changes (BL-994), check what these columns record under it before reading them. The step token is `extract.py`'s step for the asks carrying the most s321 FINISHED-INSIDE minutes (87 of 164).

Provenance: batch 254, BL-841 R1 by ruling of lead ai-dlc-7f; id allocated by ai-dlc-7f.

verify: manual

## BL-982 — `core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding` carries 30 more in-process validator runs on the suite pole since 0.1070.0, and an in-process pass is invisible to the `invocations.decl` cost guard, so a cost added that way passes every count check

**DEFECT.** Filed 2026-11-11. Two subjects. (a) The cost: 0.1070.0 added the verdict-grammar and value-citation worlds and their sixteen mutants to `core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding/run.sh` (3073 lines, `.dist-only`), and 30 of the in-process validator runs the efficiency-close block drives are theirs (27 at 0.1069.0, 57 at 0.1070.0, measured as below). The efficiency-close block (`ef_seed` at `:1717` through `ef_mutd` m-d6 at `:2314`) builds a seeded world per run, and `ef_sac_build` (`:2112`) and `ef_arch_build` (`:2219`) each make a git commit. The suite is pole-bound, so one fixture's own cost is a cost of the push. (b) The blind spot: `vin` (`core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding/run.sh:536`) sources the world's copy of `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` in a `( )` subshell and calls `vbr_main`. No bash process starts, and the counter (the `BASH_ENV` DEBUG trap armed by `scripts/validate-fixture-invocations.sh --gen-rc`) logs one row per bash START. The fixture's `invocations.decl` rows read `6151 *` and `2342 scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh`, and its own b255 F2b comment records them "unchanged, two agreeing counted runs" across the 32 added passes. So a cost added in process moves no count and passes the J3 check and every other count check.

Derived against `origin/main` at 880d4f87b (0.1071.0), each beside a control in the same invocation: `vin` is defined at `core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding/run.sh:536` (`grep -n '^vin()'` returns that one line; the same grep over a path no fixture has returns none); the fixture's `invocations.decl` carries the two rows above and the F2b comment (read from the file); `fx_inproc` has 0 callers at base.

Fix direction, built in part by the PARTIAL paragraph below: split the efficiency-close block into a sibling fixture so the old fixture's own run is shorter, count every `vin`-routed pass in a separate log the judge reads, and bind it to a declared row.

**PARTIAL (v0.1075.0) — the split, the counted in-process pass and the scoped static arm are built; the tree-wide static arm is OWED.** The headline figure is the in-process validator pass count (the `inproc:scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` row), because a counted start cannot see one. Base: a scratch copy of the push base 7ad8e93e5 (F9's code-complete tree, before the split), with `fx_inproc` called at the same four sites and run once armed, logged 174 `.inproc` rows, all naming the validator. The same run over 0.1073.0 (808918467) also logged 174; F9 changes neither the fixture nor its validator (empty `git diff --stat 808918467 7ad8e93e5` over both, against 49 files changed tree-wide). Tip: `backlog-receipt-binding` logs 117 and `backlog-receipt-efficiency` logs 57, each from two agreeing counted runs. So the old fixture fell from 174 to 117, a fall of 57, which is exactly the number of passes the efficiency-close block drove. Old plus sibling at the tip is 117 + 57 = 174, equal to base. Separately and outside the headline, the sibling's `1 inproc:core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding/run.sh` row records its one source of the old `run.sh`, a harness load and not a validator pass. Reconciled from a per-caller histogram (the scratch copy's `fx_inproc` also logged `${FUNCNAME[*]}`). The 117 that stay are `vin` called from `run_v` 57, `pr_two` 22 and `c5_run` 19, plus `r10_probe_only` 14, `r11_probe_only` 3 and `r12_probe_only` 2. The 57 that moved are `vin` called from `ef_world` 21, `ef_mutg` 7, `ef_mutd` 6, `ef_mut0` 6 and `ef_mut` 5; `ef_sac`, `ef_filing`, `ef_arch` and `ef_archmut` 2 each; and 4 at top level (VEQ-eff's two-world loop, m-nopathnarrow and m-nosacderive). The 27 comes from the same call-site count and was not run; that count reproduces the measured 57 at base and at tip. A first measurement on the pre-F11 tree (ca6a6f941, 0.1070.0) read 173 at base and 116 for the old fixture at the tip. 0.1073.0's F11 added one `run_v` pass, which accounts for the difference. The sibling's `--matrix` re-measure (28 captures x 17 arms, recorded in its header and in the split's commit) shows every deciding arm TRIPPED and its twin GREEN on all twelve. The in-process pass counts per fixture are given above. Counted starts, from two agreeing counted runs per fixture re-taken on the combined tip: `backlog-receipt-binding` reads `3305 *` / `2352` validator at base 7ad8e93e5 (the instrumented base run passed J3 against those rows) and `3303` / `2350` at the tip. `backlog-receipt-efficiency` reads `3 *` / `2`: its own `run.sh` and VEQ-eff's two `bash <copy>` cli cells. So 3305 = 3303 + 3 - 1 (the sibling's own start) and 2352 = 2350 + 2. These counts see none of the 57 passes that moved. All three hold in both fixtures. Fork TOTAL (`scripts/fork-profile.sh --section total`, the `TOTAL` line) is 2843 at the push base 7ad8e93e5 and 2843 at this tip, so FORK_BUDGET is unchanged. It read 2850 at both 808918467 and the stack over it, and 2906 at both ca6a6f941 and the counter-only tip. validator-fork-budget, in two agreeing counted runs at this tip, reads `A3 ceiling -- 2843 <= FORK_BUDGET=2926 (83 of headroom, 276 fixture dirs)`, `A3b spare -- 83 fork(s) to spare`, `A4 stale-high -- 2843 is at least 70% of 2926`, and J3 `invocation budget ok: 1111 start(s)`. The judge-runner fixtures, in two agreeing counted runs each at this tip, read J3 ok: fixture-invocation-budget 76 starts, fixture-inprocess 53, fixture-hygiene 85. The count is judged exact under J3, and (judge arm J7) `.inproc` rows logged for a fixture whose decl has no `inproc:` row FAIL. Each arm fires on a seeded offender and stays quiet on its near-miss, and a declared count off by one FAILS. Opt-in: a fixture with no `inproc:` row is judged as at base. The static half of the false-positive measurement is a byte-diff of `--static-join`, `--b2-census` and `validate-fixture-inprocess.sh` output over every fixture, base against tip. The counter hand found the three outputs byte-identical at base ca6a6f941 against the counter alone on ca6a6f941 (its tip b2519630 carries no `backlog-receipt-efficiency`): `--static-join` 540 lines, `--b2-census` 3, validate-fixture-inprocess 12. Its seeded controls differ by 4 and 6 lines, and the census moves only on an inproc-only seed. Re-taken at the push base 7ad8e93e5 against this tip, with the root path normalised: `--static-join` 544 against 546 lines. The differences are the efficiency-close block's move to `core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-efficiency` alone: the old fixture's refusal row reads 2350 starts for 2352, and the sibling adds its `F` and `P` rows. `--b2-census` and validate-fixture-inprocess differ only in the fixture count (272 to 273), the subjects rowed (427 to 429) and the predicted passes (106 to 107). No other fixture's row moved, no P1 row prints, and every exit is 0. The seeded controls ran on scratch worktrees of this tip. With `vin`'s `fx_inproc` deleted, `--static-join` differs from the tip by 1 line (the P1 row) and validate-fixture-inprocess by 3, exiting 1, while the census is unchanged. With the sibling's `0 core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding/run.sh` start row deleted, leaving only its `inproc:` row, the census differs by 3 and exits 1 (a J6 gap), validate-fixture-inprocess by 3, and `--static-join` by 0. `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh` also truncates `$FXCOUNT_DIR/$HR_FX.inproc`; it is a shipped file and is inert when the counter is unarmed, shown on a consumer tree built by `scripts/install.sh` into an empty directory (the consumer-layout inertness check of `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh`). The counter hand installed base and tip into empty directories. advisor-gate-deny, run unarmed, reads `advisor-gate-deny: PASS` with rc 0 on both sides, and 0 `*.inproc` files are created (control 1). That was taken before F11. `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh` and `core/fixtures/lib/mutant.sh` are unchanged from ca6a6f941 to 808918467 (empty `git diff --stat`; control: `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` changes over the same range), so F11 does not alter it. (judge static arm P1) In an opted-in fixture, a non-comment `.`/`source` of a path that is a declared bash subject must be paired with an `fx_inproc` call on that drive, else FAIL; its false-positive population is the six non-comment source sites in the old `run.sh` (`:264`, `:267`, `:545`, `:2557`, `:2663`, `:2699` at 7ad8e93e5), enumerated by P1's own scan. With an `inproc:` row seeded and no `fx_inproc` call, the four drives fire: `vin`'s opaque `. "$v"` and the three `r1*_probe_only` sources of the subject. The two lib sources stay quiet. The sibling adds two sites: `. "$EDIR/../lib/mutant.sh"`, which has a literal tail and stays quiet, and `. "$BRB"`, which is opaque and is paired. With the wiring in place, `--static-join` prints no P1 row for either fixture. The controls ran on scratch copies of the two fixtures. Deleting `vin`'s `fx_inproc` line gives one P1 row at the `vin` source. Deleting the sibling's gives one at the `. "$BRB"` line. The unedited copy gives 0. The suite pole is not timed here (BL-868, no timing runs): the cost of (a) is shown by the counts above, and the push's own pole line is LOADED.

**Owed:** the TREE-WIDE static arm, which makes an unrouted in-process drive of a declared bash subject fail the push in a fixture that has NOT opted in. It cannot ship in this release for two reasons. It is not opt-in, so under the lead's condition that in-process counting is opt-in it would change verdicts outside the two fixtures; and F9's in-flight in-process conversions would trip it. The population it must be measured over is large. Re-derived on a clean worktree of 880d4f87b with the census script, by the regex `(^|[;&|({ ]|then |do |else )(\.|source) +["$]` over `core/fixtures/*/run.sh` with comment lines dropped: 270 `run.sh` files, 703 sites in 209 fixtures, of which 226 name a `lib/`, `preamble`, `mutant.sh` or `matrix.sh` file and 477 in 157 fixtures do not; 233 of those 477 source a bare variable path (`. "$x"`), which a static arm cannot resolve to a subject without evaluating the fixture. Controls in the same run: the `vin` site at `backlog-receipt-binding/run.sh:541` is counted (1), an impossible token `zzqq9` returns 0. The figure is a floor of the regex's reach, not a count of in-process drives: the regex also admits `grep -c . "$x"` lines (two in the old `run.sh`, `:1293` and `:2451`), so the arm needs a grammar and its own false-positive enumeration before it can be built. Until it lands, a fixture that sources a bash subject without `fx_inproc` stays invisible to the counter outside the opted-in pair.

Receipt: `verify: manual`; the entry closes when the tree-wide arm is built with a measured false-positive set and a seeded unrouted drive in a non-opted-in fixture fails the push. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a `has` on `fx_inproc` in `validate-fixture-invocations.sh` is closable by a comment and shows only that the helper is named. What this does not establish: the wall clock of the suite pole, which this release does not time.

Outside this release's scope: item 8 of this file's real-time-waits list cites backlog-receipt-binding `run.sh:1050` for a `sleep 2` before a racing `worktree add`. ## BL-984 — nothing owns `tools.decl` completeness, so an undeclared tool silently kills a check

**DEFECT.** `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh:608` runs every fixture under `env -i PATH="$HR_BIN"`, and `$HR_BIN` (`:556-565`) is a farm of the tools that fixture's `tools.decl` declares, in both layouts. A tool the fixture calls and does not declare is `command not found`, rc 127. Where that call sits in a branch condition (`if cmp -s`, `||`, `!`, `$( )`) or under `2>/dev/null`, the 127 is read as an ordinary answer and the check it feeds stops checking, while the fixture still prints PASS. BL-983 is the measured instance: both of advisor-gate-deny's DID-NOT-APPLY guards were dead. Nothing refuses this. hermetic-run resolves DECLARED tools only, and exits 2 on one that is declared and absent. `scripts/validate-fixture-hygiene.sh` H2 covers only `command -v <declared tool>` skips. `scripts/census-hermetic-coverage.sh` is a census, not a gate, and it compares ok-line sets, so it would read advisor-gate-deny EQUAL.

Proposed mechanism, not built (lead ruling, F15): a hermetic-run.sh arm that fails a fixture whose run log carries `command not found`. Its known limits. Consumer cost: hermetic-run.sh is the shipped engine, so the arm runs on every consumer push and can fail a consumer's own fixtures. False-positive set: unmeasured, for example a fixture that deliberately runs a missing command and does not redirect it. Blind spots: it is blind to redirected stderr (`2>/dev/null`, `2>&1` into a capture), which is exactly the SWALLOWED shape. It is also blind to code that runs only on a failing branch, or only under `--matrix`, which a green run never executes. So a clean log is a FLOOR, not a proof, and the static half of the census below has to stand beside it.

Starting population (the F15 census, static candidates plus executing confirmation under hermetic-run; every instance is OPEN here, and its declaration is a later release):

CENSUS (taken at f76449762 by the F15 census hand; re-checked at the F15 tip). The method: every fixture with a static candidate, 179 of 270, was run through `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh` with a fresh mktemp verdict store, 4 in parallel. A hit counts only when execution confirms it. PLAIN means an ANSI-stripped `command not found` line in a plain run. TRAP means a BASH_ENV DEBUG trap logged a command whose first word failed `type -t`, which also catches stderr-redirected sites. FORCED means the branch was forced in a scratch copy and that copy run through hermetic-run. Controls in the same runs: advisor-gate-deny read PLAIN 2 and TRAP 2, and absorbed-specifics-survive, whose decl is complete, read PLAIN 0 and TRAP 0. The executing count is a FLOOR. NONE of the census fixtures below is declared yet: the `tools.decl` fix for them is a later release, and only advisor-gate-deny's `cmp` is fixed (BL-983). Operator ruling (b255): the declarations ship as a follow-up release, b256's opener by default.
- LOUD (PLAIN, the fixture still rc 0): advisor-gate-deny `cmp` (BL-983); prepush-ssh-keepalive `tr` (run.sh:253); readset-stage1-verdict `basename` (run.sh:262, :474, :478) and `cksum` (:466).
- FAILING-BRANCH-ONLY (FORCED): apply-legacy-script-path `diff` (run.sh:339); wait-beat-liveness `uniq` (run.sh:4450; the FAIL message loses its content).
- --MATRIX-ONLY (FORCED with MATRIX=1; 0 in a default run): retro-branch-behind-main `wc` (run.sh:474, :587); fold-architect-ledger-join `cut` (run.sh:580-581).
- SWALLOWED (TRAP, plain rc 0): escalation-citation and transient-ignore-block `md5` (`md5 -q … 2>/dev/null || md5sum`, so the before and after hashes were both empty and compared equal); pause-hook-origin `shasum` (ai-dlc-pause.sh:250, so REQ_SHA was empty); spec-join-integrity `wc` (settled-net.sh:18); dispatch-model-guard `date` and `od`; answer-handoff-routing, core-write-guard and operator-request-capture `od`, `tail` and `mv`, and upstream-routing the same plus `date` (ai-dlc-context-provenance.sh:95/120/125, `2>/dev/null || true`); askuserquestion-citation `git` (run.sh:278, `git -C … >/dev/null 2>&1` inside an `if`, BL-983's shape); adversarial-shard-merge and process-rule-pins `stat` (wait-for-deliverable.sh:528, where STAT_FLAVOR falls to gnu); apply-restamp-theirs, apply-machinery-stamp, apply-relabel-noop-row, emit-report-refusal, layer-crosswalk-home and self-update-fixture-log `uniq` (layer-drift.sh:1773); apply-relabel-noop-row `chmod` (apply.sh:571); apply-worklist-rows and layer-qualifier-grain `mv`; backlog-ledger `sort` (backlog-rotate.sh:541-542); cycle-commits-enforce `sort`; shell-portability `sort` and `paste`; adversarial-citation, backlog-real-ledger, backlog-receipt-binding and bmad-invocation-resolve `rmdir`; h2-attest-scripts-dir `seq` (check-17-bypass/run.sh:260); owed-derivations `ls`; prepush-pool-depth `diff`, `head` and `tee`; readset-skip-b `perl`; remediator-shard-join and validator-arm-selection-b `jq`; retro-branch-behind-main `find` (in a mutated copy of validate-mandatory-rules.sh); wait-beat-liveness and wait-stale-deliverable `perl`.

FIXED: advisor-gate-deny `cmp` only (BL-983). No other fixture above is fixed. A measurement for the later release: with every missing tool above declared, each fixture was re-run through hermetic-run with the invocation counter armed, and all but one read rc 0 with an unchanged budget. That one is prepush-ssh-keepalive.

OUTSIDE A PLAIN DECLARATION, and why:
- prepush-ssh-keepalive `tr`: OPEN. With `tr` declared the fixture prints `ok (19 assertions)`, but the invocation judge fails it: `FAIL (J3): row "19 *": measured 22, declared 19`. The 3 extra starts are attributed, unverified, to the `$( )` sites at run.sh:258, :279 and :282, which run once `tr` resolves.
- Already declared, so the hit comes from a site that sets its own PATH, not from tools.decl: wait-beat-liveness `perl` (run.sh:2861 deliberately hides perl from a constructed PATH), readset-skip-b `perl`, remediator-shard-join `jq`, and core-write-guard, operator-request-capture and upstream-routing `tail`.
- readset-stage1-verdict `rs_sleep`, a side finding: noted, not a close condition of this entry. It is a function (`core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh:1778`), not a tool. A generated deriver.sh calls it and never defines it, so a plain run prints 6 `rs_sleep: command not found` lines.

LEFT OPEN:
- `xcode-select` in readset-skip-b: macOS-only, with 0 precedent declarations.
- `claude` in shipped-rule-version-floor: deliberate, and in READSET_UNKEYED_TOOLS.
- `pgrep` in consumer-suite-pool: unchecked.
- `md5sum` and `sha256sum`: second legs of alternations, left undeclared. Precedent declares the first leg (`md5` in 18 fixtures, `shasum` in 81).
- hook-registration-join and retro-compliance-workflow: no tools.decl and no inputs.decl, so hermetic-run refuses them with rc 2 and nothing was executed.
- 94 fixtures were scanned statically only (0 hits), which is not an executing proof.
- 7 fixtures set `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:…` and are unaudited.
- The trap changed the verdict of 14 fixtures, so their tags come from plain runs only.
- Generated-at-runtime .sh files were only partly scanned.
- False positive: fanout-untracked-corpus's only `cmp` is a comment (run.sh:192).
- The executing count is a FLOOR.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. No runner-wired receipt exists for an unbuilt hermetic-run arm. A `has` on `hermetic-run.sh` for `command not found` is closable by a comment. A fixture arm that seeds a fixture calling an undeclared tool and asserts hermetic-run refuses it is the receipt shape, and it lands with the arm. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: declaring every tool the census found, which fixes today's population and leaves the next undeclared tool as silent as this one.

Provenance: batch 255, release F15 (BL-983's population question); id allocated by lead ruling.

verify: manual

## BL-985 — prepush-pool-depth's watcher drive goes FIXTURE BROKEN under load, a verdict that moves with load (BL-868)

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. The emitter is pn_settle's safety bound in `core/fixtures/prepush-pool-depth/run.sh`: line 834 is `broken "watcher drive $t: neither a verdict nor a FIFO open after $PD_REJ_MAX rejected settles of one table ($lr)"`, with `PD_REJ_MAX=200` at `:96`. pn_settle (`:824`) calls pd_settle (`:195`) at `:829`. pd_settle's own header (`:190-194`) says it ends on an event or a settle and "never on a count of polls or on elapsed time", but each settle is paced by `sleep 0.1`, so 200 consecutive rejections of one unchanged table is in effect a wall-clock bound that load can exhaust. The barrier drive has the twin bound at `:291` on the same PD_REJ_MAX, reported by hang_check at `:352`.

Instance 1, from a local failure copy that is not in-tree (`.git/ai-dlc-fixture-failures.20261112T081548Z.11581`): F14's counted whole-suite push of 87dcbb935, pool width 12, 1 of 271 units red. `FAIL  watcher drive pn.m-novalidate_hout: neither a verdict nor a FIFO open after 200 rejected settles of one table (S:65292:S,67664:S,67686:S,67817:S,67828:S,)`, then `prepush-pool-depth: FIXTURE BROKEN`; runner `hermetic-run: prepush-pool-depth: rc=2 sandbox_files=51 required_missing=0`; teardown `rm: …/hermetic-run.4m8Ezr/tmp/prepush-pool-depth.t6DWC7: Directory not empty`. F14's causal check: none of its changes are on this fixture's path unarmed; armed, they add only one empty-file truncate before start. The re-push of the same sha re-ran only prepush-pool-depth and it read `ok`: it passed on re-run of the same sha.

Never a retry, a widened bound or fewer jobs (BL-868). ## BL-1000 — I75's fails-closed check reads the whole AI_DLC_ROOT span, so in merge-review-shards.sh any `exit N` in a 1381-line body satisfies it and the guard passes with its fails-closed line deleted

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. I75 (`scripts/validate-enforcement-map.sh:6494`) asserts that every root-consulting `core/scripts/*.sh` fails closed. Its reader is `i75_failsclosed` (`:6548`): `i75_norm "$1" | awk '/exit [0-9]|:-\/nonexistent/ { f = 1 } …'`. `i75_norm` (`:6527`) is the awk range `/^# --- AI_DLC_ROOT ---/,/^# --- end AI_DLC_ROOT ---/` with comment and blank lines removed. So the token is bound to the whole span between the markers, not to the chain's terminal guard. Where the span is the block (a few dozen lines), that is the same thing. Where it is not, any `exit N` in the span acquits the subject.

Measured at the release tree (f11a8e566 plus this release) (the arm's own subject grep, `grep -lE 'AI_DLC_PROJECT_ROOT|CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR|ai_dlc_resolve_root' core/scripts/*.sh`, 41 subjects):
- `core/scripts/merge-review-shards.sh`: the start marker is `:557` `# --- AI_DLC_ROOT ---…` and the end marker is `:1937` `# --- end AI_DLC_ROOT ---…`, a span of 1381 lines. The resolver function sits at `:562-571` and the chain at `:1927-1936`, and the program body between them is inside the span. 10 non-comment lines in the span match the arm's alternations (`:785`, `:788`, `:789`, `:790`, `:953`, `:1803`, `:1823`, `:1846`, `:1849`, `:1935`). One of them, `:1935` `  exit 2` inside the `[ -n "$AI_DLC_ROOT" ] || {` guard, is the fails-closed site.
- `core/scripts/audit-layer-debt.sh`: start marker `:54`, NO end marker, so the range runs to EOF (`:730`), 677 lines. Its chain (`:69-73`) has no terminal root guard. The one line I75 accepts is `:82` `exit 2`, the absent-register guard (`if [ ! -f "$REGISTER" ]`), not a root guard. A second instance of the same defect, found statically and not probed.
- Every other subject: span 22-42 lines, exactly 1 matching line. Control: `core/scripts/check-owed-derivations.sh`, markers `:119`/`:140`, span 22 lines, matching line `:139`.

Executed probe, in a `git archive HEAD` scratch copy under mktemp, never the real tree. Each copy's own `scripts/validate-enforcement-map.sh --arms I75` was run with `AI_DLC_PROJECT_ROOT` set to that copy:
- unmodified copy: rc 0, 0 I75 lines.
- merge-review-shards.sh `:1935` (`exit 2`) deleted, with `cmp` against the original confirming the file differs (2480 -> 2479 lines): rc 0, 0 I75 lines, no FAIL. The defect: the fails-closed line is gone and I75 reads green.
- check-owed-derivations.sh `:139` deleted: rc 1, `FAIL: I75 script(s) whose root resolution does not fail closed: check-owed-derivations.sh`, drift FAIL 0. So the arm fires on a small span, and the large-span result is not a dead arm.

Fix direction: bind the token to the block, not the file body. Read the fails-closed token only from the chain's terminal guard (the lines after the cwd-walk line, up to the end marker), and fail a subject whose start marker has no matching end marker. That fix turns I75 red on today's tree, so the same change gives `audit-layer-debt.sh` an end marker and a terminal root guard.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The closing receipt is a fixture arm or a vem self-probe in which deleting a large-span subject's fails-closed line turns I75 red, and in which a start marker with no end marker turns I75 red as well. Both inputs have to be real-sized spans, not a synthetic block of a few lines. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: the existing self-probe (`:6581-6585`), a synthetic block of four lines, which the current arm already passes.

Provenance: batch 256, b256-C6 delta adversary NOTE; id allocated by lead ai-dlc-a7.

verify: manual

## BL-997 — retro's `git checkout main && git merge --ff-only origin/main` chain silently overwrites an operator's IGNORED file at a path origin/main tracks, and exits 0

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. The chain `git checkout main && git merge --ff-only origin/main` runs in `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/retro.md` Step 1 (`:27` at base e3d2dfc35 and at the release tip) and in the 7a-post step 1 LAND block (base `:1340`; at the tip the fenced block at `:1434-1435`). When an operator holds an IGNORED file at a path that origin/main force-adds, the fast-forward replaces the file with the upstream bytes and exits 0. At the tip the LAND block then prints its success line `LAND: main at origin/main` over the loss; BL-990's release adds no behaviour change here. An untracked, non-ignored file at such a path is protected, because the fast-forward aborts with exit 1.

Measured worlds: ignored `e.log`, `é.log` and `sub/x.log`, each force-added upstream, all clobbered from the operator's data to the upstream bytes by base `:1340`, by the tip LAND block, and by `:27` at base and at the tip. Controls in the same runs: a no-op subject keeps the operator's data; `git check-ignore e.log` reports the file ignored while `e.log` is tracked at origin/main; `grep ff-only` hits 2 in both files. git 2.56.0. That `gh pr merge --delete-branch`'s own local step is the same chain is an inference from the gh v2.103.3 source, not run.

Owed: a pre-merge check that refuses, before the fast-forward, when a path the incoming range adds is ignored and present in the working tree. BL-986 (C2's sibling, the update skill's return, where C2 is adding a pre-merge check) is the mechanism to reuse rather than growing a second. No fix in this release; the fix owner is assigned later.

Not built, and nothing runnable exists to point a receipt at: no check mechanism exists yet, and a `has` on `retro.md` for a check name is closable by a comment. The receipt shape when it lands is a fixture world in `core/fixtures/retro-branch-behind-main` holding an ignored file at a path origin/main force-adds, in which the Step 1 block and the LAND block both refuse and the file's bytes survive.

Provenance: batch 256, cluster N9; id allocated by the lead.

## BL-998 — both pre-push hooks print only `ok <name>` for a green fixture, so a PENDING row never reaches a consumer's operator

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. A shipped fixture whose subject is absent prints a `PENDING` row and exits 0 (BL-990's `retro-branch-behind-main` is the worked case). The hook that runs it prints, for any fixture whose verdict is `ok`, only `   ok    <name>`: `core/git-hooks/pre-push:2429` and `.githooks/pre-push:3063` (both `if [ "$verdict" = ok ]; then printf '   ok    %s\n' "$b"`, at this release's tip). The fixture's own output is shown only for a non-ok verdict: `core/git-hooks/pre-push:2523-2527` skips an ok unit (`[ -f "$out/$b" ] && [ "$(cat "$out/$b")" = ok ] && continue`) and prints the `===== <name> =====` log block for the rest. So a fixture that checked nothing because its subject is absent reads the same as one that passed.

Population, at this release's tip: `/usr/bin/grep -lE "PENDING  " core/fixtures/*/run.sh` lists 5 fixtures, all shipping (none carries `.dist-only`); the present-token control `grep -c "PENDING  "` on `retro-branch-behind-main/run.sh` reads 3 and an impossible token reads 0. Three of the five emit the row with an `echo` or `printf` (`pause-hook-origin`, `push-drain-refusals`, `retro-branch-behind-main`); `check-22-spawn-ledger` and `check-24-adversarial-convergence` match on lines that are not an `echo` or `printf`, and were not inspected further. `retro-branch-behind-main` prints one PENDING row per absent retro.md block on a consumer and self-clears: its `inputs.decl` declares `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/retro.md`, so its key moves when retro.md lands.

Owed: the hook surfaces a PENDING count (the number of green fixtures that printed a `PENDING` row), in both hooks.

Not built, and no runner-wired receipt is honestly constructible now: the surfacing mechanism does not exist, and a `has` on either hook for `PENDING` is closable by a comment.

## BL-996 — a sharded gate repair with no serial round leaves the remediation guard no record to lift on

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. Premise, each derived at this entry's fold tip:
- `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_gate-procedures.md:1092-1133` ("Every escalated entry reaches the serial remediator") dispatches serial remediators only "whenever SERIAL-REQUIRED or ESCALATED-COUNT is above 0" (`:1097-1098`): one lane remediator per lane, in parallel (`:1119`), then one fan-in remediator (`:1130-1131`). When the join prints `SERIAL-REQUIRED: 0` and `ESCALATED-COUNT: 0`, no serial remediator runs.
- A shard writes no agent id (`core/team-roles/remediator.md` item 1), so the join's record carries none. `_gate-procedures.md:1337` ("At a gate failure, give the remediator its own agent id") gives the gate nonce and an id to ONE serial-round remediator, the fan-in remediator when the round-one join prints `FANIN-COUNT:` above 0 and otherwise the lane-1 remediator, and only that one writes `gate-adjudication/<nonce>.repair.md`. With no serial round, no agent receives an id and nobody writes that file.
- LIFT ARM 8 of `core/hooks/ai-dlc-gate-remediation-guard.sh` (header `:1856`, the two candidate homes named at `:1858-1859` and read at `:1891-1892`, cited at v0.1083.0) lifts only on a record bound to the live nonce that carries a non-empty `remediator_agent_id:` and a `repaired_checks:` naming a live FAIL. In this case neither home holds such a record, so arm 8 has nothing to lift on.
- The deny then stands until a re-run of the failed check (`steps/gate-validation.md` "Gate Failure", step 2) writes a newest pass bound to a dispatch that records no FAIL (hook decision order `:69-75`: "a clean bound pass allows"), every FAIL is covered by an in-force SUPPRESSED entry or a verified waiver (`:80-83`, arm 7b), or an operator authorization lifts it (LIFT ARM 9, `:1974`). `remediator.md`'s paragraph **A gate repair record carries the live gate nonce** says that when the join prints `SERIAL-REQUIRED: 0` and `ESCALATED-COUNT: 0`, no serial remediator runs, nobody writes `gate-adjudication/<nonce>.repair.md`, and the guard has no record to lift on; `_gate-procedures.md:1337` says the same and names the three exits above. No mechanism gives the honest sharded repair a lift of its own.

Measured by a measurement hand, driving the REAL hook. The world was built with the real `core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh` on the `p_sjgate` path of `core/fixtures/remediator-shard-join/lib.sh` (`:769`), every shard part `edit: n/a`, so the join printed `SERIAL-REQUIRED: 0`, `ESCALATED-COUNT: 0`, `FANIN-COUNT: 0` and `LANE-COUNT: 0`. The joined record `planning-artifacts/s<N>/gate-<type>-repair-p<M>.md` carries no gate nonce: the join copies each part verbatim under a `<!-- shard: X -->` marker (`join-remediator-shards.sh:3452-3494`), so arm 8 skips it at the nonce bind. On byte-identical worlds the hook at base f11a8e566 read DENY, and at the tip DENY, so this release adds no new refusal. Controls at both revisions: no record read DENY, and a genuine column-0 `gate-adjudication/<nonce>.repair.md` read ALLOW. Frequency on the reference consumer (graph main 6ee289617, `git log --all --name-only`): 3 sharded gate repairs, all sprint 319 (`gate-implementation-repair-p3`, `-p4` and `-p5`), against a control of 158 `shards/*repair*` directories and 0 for an impossible token. 0 of the 3 are in this state; each ends in `repair-complete: serial`. The state is reachable by the procedure and has not occurred.

No fix is proposed.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. No runner-wired receipt exists for an unchosen fix. A `has` on either file for the sentence describing this case is closable by that sentence, which this entry's own release already wrote. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: an operator authorization or a clean re-run, both of which lift today and leave the defect in place.

## BL-1001 — `audit-rule-files.sh`'s habitual `used to` detector acquits a history clause whose subject carries an indefinite article

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. Two history clauses in resident rule prose read as habitual past and were not flagged by the audit's NARRATIVE_DRIFT class: `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/requirements.md` "Where a per-sprint block used to sit, write:" and `core/skills/ai-dlc-update/SKILL.md` "a failed push used to commit locally". Both are rewritten by BL-991 in this release.

The grammar that missed them is clause 2 of `PURPOSE_BEFORE` in `core/scripts/audit-rule-files.sh`, `\b(a|an)\b(\s+[^\s.;:!?]+){0,3}\s*$`. It reads any indefinite article within four words before `used to` as a reduced-relative purpose clause ("a health signal used to clear the operation") and clears the line. The script's own header comment names this as a stated limit ("a habitual clause with an indefinite subject ... clears"); these are two measured real instances.

One count, over the audit's `--list` corpus (88 files, `bash core/scripts/audit-rule-files.sh --list`): the lines where `used to` follows `a` or `an` within four words, regex `\b(a|an)\b(\s+[^\s.;:!?]+){0,3}\s+used to\b` case-insensitive, same-line. At `e3d2dfc35` (the file list taken from this release's tip, the text read at that revision) the count is 3, and it includes the two cited lines (the positive control: `requirements.md:114` and `SKILL.md:455` both appear; `git show e3d2dfc35:<file> | grep -c` reads 1 for `used to sit` in `requirements.md` and 1 for `push used to` in `SKILL.md`, against 0 for an impossible token); at this release's tip it is 1; the impossible-token control (the same regex with `zzqx9never` before `used to`) reads 0. The count covers purpose clauses and habitual clauses alike, so it is an upper bound on the habitual ones.

Not fixed here, and no other instance was hunted.

Provenance: batch 256, cluster N13; id allocated by the lead.

## BL-1011 — the party-wave-barrier detector's per-segment WAIT misses four wait spellings in a followed coordinator file, so a barrier written that way is acquitted

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. In a followed coordinator file, `scripts/audit-party-wave-barrier.sh` reads a command segment as a wait through its `iswait` and `wlead` definitions. Four spellings are not read as waits: `if <wait> ...; then`, `until <wait> ...; do`, `r=$(<wait> ...)` and `x=$(sleep N)`. `wlead` strips `do`, `then`, `else` and `elif` from the head of a segment but not `if`, `while` or `until`, and `NAME=$(cmd` reads as one assignment token. So the response to a delivery runs on to a later dispatch, and a real barrier written in one of these forms is acquitted. It was found by BL-695's tip adversary on seeded copies of seed u. No rule, team role or core script prescribes these forms: `core/skills`, `core/rules`, `core/team-roles` and `core/scripts` were searched.

Corpus exposure, measured read-only over graph's transcripts with the committed detector's own WAIT definitions:
- The 10 followed coordinator files hold 447 foreground Bash commands, of which the shipped rule classes 38 as waits (the control). 0 are in an acquitting shape: the one `if` hit wraps a `pgrep` probe inside a loop the rule already classes as a wait.
- Over all 6158 subagent files there are 4 candidate hits: 2 are `pgrep` probes, 1 is the coordinator hit above, and 1 is unresolved (a non-dispatching subagent file, which the detector never follows).
- Lead files never use this rule.

Fix: add `if`, `while` and `until` to `wlead`'s keyword strip and split segments on `$(` so the inner command is read; then add an arm and a mutant per shape.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. No runner-wired receipt exists for an unchosen fix.

Provenance: batch 256, release cluster b256-N6, from BL-695's tip adversary; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual

## BL-1015 — the s322 lead never ran `validate-held-stories.sh` at the sub-step snapshot site, where the step text runs it after every story transition

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. Graph's installed `.claude/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_gate-procedures.md:50`, inside "Sub-step snapshot update", which `implementation.md:676-683` requires "after every story transition", says unconditionally: "Run `scripts/ai-dlc/validate-held-stories.sh`. On exit 1, dispatch or hold each UNACCOUNTED story before the next wave…". In s322 the lead made 145 `snapshot-update.sh --activity` writes (an upper bound on reaches of that procedure; 106 name a story transition, the procedure's trigger at `_gate-procedures.md:18-19`) and ran the validator there 0 times. Its only run was at the `implementation.md:726` exit site, 2026-11-12T03:32:49Z in e444bf13, output `held-stories: OK -- 8 stories, every ready-for-dev story held or in flight`.

Derivation, graph pinned at `ed61de745`, read only: lead-only (non-sidechain) Bash tool_uses in the top-level `~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph/*.jsonl`, timestamped records only, deduped on `tool_use.id`.
- `--activity` writes are counted as one `snapshot-update.sh` call carrying `--activity` per tool_use, with back-to-back retries removed and calls through a shell variable (`S=scripts/ai-dlc/snapshot-update.sh; $S --activity`) resolved. s322 gives 145; 167 raw; 106 under a strict reading where the activity names a story and a transition. The count is an upper bound on reaches of the procedure, and the strict count is the one keyed on its trigger.
- Validator runs are counted at command position, splitting each command on `;`, newline, `&&`, `||` and `|`.
- Population: the implementation windows, s321 2026-11-06T03:26:40Z to 2026-11-07T11:42:56Z (lead transcripts 0ec8b044, a7355a05, f369e605, 4f12d1d9) and s322 2026-11-10T18:47:12Z to 2026-11-12T03:35:48Z (lead transcripts fdc4249a, 70fd1d6d, 6249c621, e444bf13). The other transcripts in those windows (an ai-dlc-update session and a hook-error chat) carry 0 snapshot calls and 0 runs. Graph transcripts older than 2026-10-07T08:00:53Z are no longer on disk, so no earlier sprint can be counted.

Controls, same invocation: s321's lead made 226 `snapshot-update.sh --activity` writes (168 naming a story transition) and ran the validator 98 times: 88 at the sub-step site (in the same Bash call as a `snapshot-update.sh` invocation, or within 3 lead calls of an `--activity` write), 1 at the exit site and 9 unattributed. The positive controls reproduce: the s322 exit-site run above, and s321 lead 0ec8b044's 5 runs (2026-11-06T06:30:19Z, 06:31:04Z, 10:59:01Z, 10:59:14Z, 10:59:27Z). Impossible token `ZQXV-held-never-9471` reads 0. The top-level files hold 0 sidechain records, so sidechain work is read from `*/subagents/*.jsonl`: across s322's 16,293 sidechain records the validator is mentioned 0 times, against a positive control of 2 mentions in s321's subagent tool_results (2026-11-06T07:44:14Z and 17:27:39Z). Across every tool_use type and tool_result, top-level and subagent files alike, the validator's own output line `held-stories: ` appears once in s322 (the 03:32:49Z exit-site run) and 97 times in s321, against the 98 s321 runs above. Nothing in graph's `scripts/`, `.claude/hooks` or `settings.json` invokes the validator at `768cfa059` or at `ed61de745`.

s322 was not single-wave. A 5-story wave dispatched at 2026-11-10T18:49:53Z was killed by a host crash and re-dispatched at 2026-11-11T04:17Z; stories 1.6 to 1.8 were authored and run serially; a later wave ran 4 seats. Of those 14, the s322 lead's are 6c14d949 (the sprint start: its first user message, at 2026-11-09T00:01:21Z, is an `/ai-dlc` invocation that loads the orchestrator skill), fdc4249a, 70fd1d6d, 6249c621 and e444bf13; the rest include non-lead sessions (an ai-dlc-update session and a hook-error chat), and the other nine were not classified one by one.

Re-reads: s321's lead had one in-window Bash re-read covering `:50`, a7355a05 at 2026-11-06T15:43:51Z, `sed -n 1,60p .claude/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_gate-procedures.md | cut -c1-200`, whose result carries the `:50` text. ## BL-1003 — the extractor's gate span ends only on `gate-checkpoint.sh … close`, so a gate the lead never closes charges every later step's spawns to `gate-validation[<step>]`

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. There is no graph pipeline step; the cost lives in `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py`, analysis tooling, and in every reading taken with it.

Mechanism, read at this entry's base. In `extract.py`, `gate_event()` returns `close` for a `gate-checkpoint.sh` invocation only on `if "close" in args: ev = "close"`, and `open` only on `elif "open" in args: ev = "open"`. Every other verb returns None. The lead scan sets the label on `if ev == "open": gate = "gate-validation[" + main + "]"; step = gate` and clears it only on `elif ev == "close": gate = None; step = main`. While a gate is open, a whole read of another step file moves `main` but not `step` (the module docstring's "Gate span" paragraph). So a gate that is opened and never closed keeps its label until the next `open` or `close`, and every spawn in between joins to it.

The script's own verbs. `core/scripts/gate-checkpoint.sh` does NOT define `done` as a closing verb. Its usage block glosses `done` as `# comma list for gate-slice --done` and `close` as `# gate finished: no gate is live`. The `close)` arm is the only one that removes the OPEN marker (`rm -f "$OPEN_MARKER"`); the `done)` arm only prints the PASS and SKIP check ids from the ledger. `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/gate-validation.md` calls `done` inside a live gate, on a post-compaction resume pass (`--done "$(scripts/ai-dlc/gate-checkpoint.sh --nonce <gate_nonce> done)"`), and orders the close separately ("After Check 15, run `gate-checkpoint.sh --nonce <gate_nonce> close`"). So adding `done` to the close set is not the fix, because it would end a live gate's span at every resume. `gate-transition.sh <story> close` does not end a gate either: its header says that arm reads the gate-3 ledger "through gate-checkpoint.sh list, and writes a close plan", and the script's only `gate-checkpoint.sh` calls are `open` and `list`. So `gate_event()` ignoring that verb (it maps only a second positional of `3`) matches the script. The extractor reads both scripts' verbs correctly. What it lacks is a terminator for a gate the lead leaves open.

Measured on graph s322 by a peer hand's re-extraction (EXTRACT_WIN_END=2026-11-12T18:53:06Z, split by owner over that run's `spawns.jsonl`). This filing ran nothing and did not re-derive these figures. The s322 lead (session 6c14d949) opened the planning gate at nonce `planning-20261109T134029Z`. It ran `gate-checkpoint.sh … done` at 13:53:46Z and 13:55:00Z, ran no `close` between 13:30Z and 20:30Z, and read `requirements.md` from 14:00:05Z. 81 spawns, $174.47 and 27.19 agent-h carry `gate-validation[carry-over-evaluation]`. Of them, 20 (13:46Z to 13:52Z: the planning-gate adjudicator and re-adjudicate shards 1 to 10 of 10) are genuine planning-gate work, $7.06 and 0.37 agent-h. The other 61 (14:00Z to 19:08Z) are requirements-step work, mis-labelled: $167.41 and 26.82 agent-h. Controls in that run: s319 carry-over-evaluation re-derives 88 spawns and $86.66, BL-812's recorded figure, and an impossible step reads 0. The token control was not checked. Graph's committed records do not independently confirm the timeline (the clone at graph 1449b5c72 carries no 2026-11-09 commit), so the 14:00Z boundary rests on the transcript alone.

Not filed here, for the lead: the same transcript also reads as a pipeline defect. The lead skipped `gate-validation.md`'s `close`, so the planning gate's OPEN marker stood through the requirements step, and `gate-checkpoint.sh current` would have answered that nonce. Whether the marker was closed later was not read.

Suspect readings. The open..close rule entered `extract.py` in 0.1026.0 (BL-927, 2026-11-06; `git log -S'"close" in args'` on the file returns that release alone). Each paragraph's date below is the commit that first carries a distinctive phrase of it (`git log -S'<phrase>' -- <file>`, oldest hit; an impossible phrase returns 0). Each reading listed was taken after 0.1026.0 and counts a `gate-validation[<step>]` label, so each one moves if any lead in its population left a gate open:
- `docs/backlog.md` BL-808, "Outcome reading (PARTIAL, batch b255)": implementation plus `gate-validation[implementation]` per story, s319 to s321, EXTRACT_WIN_END=2026-11-11T10:30:00Z.

- BL-812, "Progress (batch b256)": the sprint-review "both labels" figures, which count `gate-validation[sprint-review]` into the step (s319 75 spawns, $60.49; s321 4 spawns, $2.78). The s321 sprint-review reading (BL-1004) is exposed the same way: a sprint-review gate left open would charge the following step's work to a label that reading counts as the step's.
- BL-812, "Projection (c), carry-over-evaluation, against s322": the step population EXCLUDES `gate-validation[carry-over-evaluation]` (81 spawns, $174.47, 27.19 h) on an inference. The split above replaces the inference: 20 spawns and $7.06 belong to the step's gate, and 61 spawns and $167.41 to requirements.

## BL-1004 — s321 sprint-review's pm seats made 0 of the step's 15 unique survivors, at $17.00

**NOTE (efficiency).** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. This is ONE sprint's reading: graph s321, from `docs/analysis/step-value-sprint-review-deploy-validate-s321.md`, by the method of `docs/analysis/step-value-s317-s319.md`, with every count and dollar figure from `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/sr_dv_yield.py` (EXTRACT_WIN_END=2026-11-09T00:00:00Z). Over the labels `sprint-review` and `gate-validation[sprint-review]`, the step ran 169 spawns for $209.09 and made 15 distinct step-unique survivors ($13.94 each), 3 of them high-consequence (D2, D4, D8). The pm seats (sonnet, 26 seats; s319: 26, $11.02) cost $17.00 and made none of the 15. Their one MAJOR, pm-02#F-1, is D1, which is counted REFOUND (open). The document reads the step as KEEP and records the pm seats as a narrowing candidate that one sprint does not decide.

Hours: the pm seats are 26 spawns, $17.00 and 0.75 agent-h. That figure is agent-hours (the spawns' own `minutes` summed and divided by 60), not wall clock. The document gives no hours for the pm seats, because `sr_dv_yield.py` prints the seat block as spawns and dollars by (role, model ran) and prints agent-minutes only per phase. Derivation: the release owner's query over the re-extracted `spawns.jsonl` (EXTRACT_WIN_END=2026-11-12T18:53:06Z), filtered to sprint 321, step `sprint-review` and role `pm`. Control: the $17.00 matches the document's pm row, and the step's other roles sum to the document's step total. This filing ran nothing.

The population counts `gate-validation[sprint-review]` into the step, so this reading is one of BL-1003's suspect readings.

What decides it: the same reading on the next measured graph sprint, by the same method and script, giving the pm seats' step-unique survivors, their dollars and their hours. Remove the pm seats from sprint-review if that sprint also reads 0 step-unique survivors from them and no high-consequence catch is theirs alone. Keep them if they make at least one step-unique survivor at a cost per survivor no higher than the step's own on that sprint. Keep them whatever the count on any high-consequence step-unique catch that no other seat raised: the high-consequence guard judges a rare catch on its consequence, not its frequency.

Baseline: s321
Step: sprint-review
Expected movement: if the second reading also reads 0, removing the pm seats takes about 26 spawns and $17.00 a sprint out of sprint-review ($0.68 per story at s321's 25 stories), and the step's step-unique survivor count stays at what the other seats made.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`), with its high-consequence guard.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The close is a measurement on a future sprint, so no tree receipt exists. The p2 and p3 passes as a whole read KEEP ("Keep while repairs write defects"): all 6 of their MAJORs sit in text the previous repair wrote, 4 of them on the native-spend bound. The document records the unchanged-text shards as a narrow candidate only, because 19 reads in one sprint rule out a frequent catch and not a rare high-consequence one.

Candidate change, not built: narrow p2 and p3 to the stories whose text changed since the previous pass, by the same `artifact_sha` comparison. A story whose bytes the prior pass already read is not re-dispatched.

What decides it: the same count on the next measured graph sprint, by the same script: the p2 and p3 shards whose `artifact_sha` equals the prior pass's, and the CRITICAL or MAJOR findings they raise, with dollars and spawn-hours. Keep it also on any high-consequence catch from those shards, whatever the count, under the high-consequence guard. A catch that depends on another story's changed text, read through an unchanged story, is the case to look for, because the narrowing would drop it.

Step: stories-test-strategy
Expected movement: p2 and p3 adversary shards on stories with unchanged text fall from 19 on s321 to 0, removing up to $7.57 and 0.48 spawn-hours a sprint at s321's shape, while the p2 and p3 MAJORs raised from changed-text shards are unchanged.

## BL-1009 — the lead can leave a gate without `gate-checkpoint.sh … close`, so the OPEN marker outlives the gate and a compaction resumes a finished gate

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. No fix in this release. `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/gate-validation.md` orders the close in prose: "After Check 15, run `gate-checkpoint.sh --nonce <gate_nonce> close`". `core/skills/ai-dlc/SKILL.md` says the same ("The gate is `open`ed at nonce mint and `close`d after Check 15"). Nothing enforces it. No later step's entry reads the marker, and `gate-checkpoint.sh`'s `open)` arm overwrites an existing marker without refusing.

What reads the OPEN marker (`OPEN_MARKER="${LEDGER_DIR}/OPEN"` in `core/scripts/gate-checkpoint.sh`), from a grep of `core/` for `gate-checkpoint/OPEN` and for `gate-checkpoint.sh current`. Outside fixtures and `SKILL.md`'s prose, there are two readers, both in `core/` (the first two bullets); the third bullet is the analysis extractor, which is not a `core/` reader:
- `core/hooks/ai-dlc-recover.sh`, the post-compaction hook. It runs `bash "$_ckp" current` into `GATE_NONCE`, and when that is non-empty it injects "## The cut landed inside a gate -- RESUME it, do not restart it", naming `.gate-checkpoint/OPEN`'s nonce and the `gate-slice.sh … --done` command for it. With a stale marker this sends a lead that has left the gate back into it, at a finished gate's nonce. That is a mis-route, so this is a DEFECT and not a NOTE.
- `core/hooks/ai-dlc-dispatch-guard.sh`, check (e), "the gate-3 adjudicator: the live OPEN marker carries the plan's nonce". It denies a `gate-adjudicator` dispatch unless `.gate-checkpoint/OPEN` equals the plan's nonce. A stale marker causes no refusal here, because `gate-transition.sh <story> 3` opens a fresh nonce, which overwrites the marker before the adjudicator is dispatched. No consequence beyond the recover hook's was found.
- The analysis extractor's mislabelled spawns are BL-1003, the same omission read from the other side.

s322, re-derived by this hand from graph's lead transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph/*.jsonl`, the set `extract.py` reads), with `extract.py`'s own `gate_event()` and `EDGES`:
- Session 6c14d949 opened a gate at 13:40:29Z on 2026-11-09 (`--nonce "$N"`; the peer hand's findings in `findings-coe322.md` give `$N` as `planning-20261109T134029Z`). It opened again at 13:49:03Z (`$N2`), a re-dispatch. The `done` call at 13:53:46Z and the recover hook's 13:50:46Z injection both name `planning-20261109T134906Z`, so the marker held the re-dispatch nonce from 13:49Z on. The second `done` is at 13:55:00Z (`$N`).
- That session made 0 `gate-checkpoint.sh … close` calls between 13:30Z and 20:30Z. The next gate event in any lead (an open or close) is at 2026-11-10T02:28:08Z.
- `steps/requirements.md` was first read at 14:00:05Z (any Read) and first read whole at 15:08:37Z.
- So the marker stood for about 11 h through the requirements step. No recover-hook injection fell in that window, so on s322 the consequence was latent.
- A literal-nonce search for `planning-20261109T134029Z` reads 0 in the lead commands, because the lead passed the nonce as `$N`. That is a property of the commands, not an absence.

s317 to s322, by the same scan. An episode runs from an open with no gate live to the next close; re-dispatch opens inside an episode count as opens. Carried means the lead read another step file whole before any close. The marker is modelled globally, in time order across lead sessions, as it is on disk.

| Sprint | opens | closes | episodes | carried into another step |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| s317 | 27 | 19 | 19 | 1 (implementation to handoff, 562ac82a, stale about 5.3 h) |
| s318 | 16 | 10 | 10 | 2 (architecture to stories-test-strategy, 3 s; sprint-review to handoff, 20bd359c, about 3.3 h) |
| s319 | 23 | 15 | 13 | 0 |
| s320 | 18 | 16 | 15 | 0 |
| s321 | 40 | 29 | 27 | 1 (architecture to stories-test-strategy, 189712ad, about 1.7 min) |
| s322 | 33 | 20 | 20 | 3 (carry-over-evaluation's planning gate to requirements, 6c14d949, about 11 h; architecture to stories-test-strategy, 14 s; sprint-review to deploy-validate, e444bf13, about 11 min) |

Controls in the same invocations: the must-hit, s322's unclosed planning gate, appears as carried; the impossible verb `zzqneververb` reads 0 `gate-checkpoint.sh` invocations. The consequence has occurred once. In s318, session 20bd359c's recover hook injected "The cut landed inside a gate -- RESUME it" at 2026-10-14T12:18:03Z for `sprint-review-20261014T105054Z`, about an hour after that lead had moved to the handoff step (11:10:05Z), with no close in between. Two further hits inside carried windows are `api-request-blob` records within a second of the carrying read. They carry earlier context and are not new injections.

Owed, not designed here: a mechanism that carries the duty to close, so it no longer rests on prose. The candidate home is the NEXT step's entry: it refuses while `.gate-checkpoint/OPEN` names a gate the lead has left, or closes that stale marker. The recover hook's resume block, which trusts the marker, is the reader the fix must protect. BL-1003 is the extractor-side defect from the same omission.

Step: gate-validation[carry-over-evaluation]

Bare `gate-validation` is refused by `estep()` in `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` (`if (t == "gate-validation") return 0`), so the one token is the measured instance's gate label. The duty sits in `gate-validation.md` and binds every gate type.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. No mechanism is chosen, so nothing exists for an `sh` receipt to run, and a `has` on a step file for a close instruction is closable by prose that already exists. The closing receipt is a fixture world in which a gate is opened and never closed, the next step is entered, and either the entry refuses or the marker is gone before the recover hook runs. In the same world, the recover hook must inject no "RESUME it" block naming the left gate. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a louder sentence in `gate-validation.md`, which is the carrier that already failed 7 times in s317 to s322.

## BL-994 — whether the Claude Code harness offers a non-blocking ask is unmeasured, and BL-972's option 2 depends on it

**NOTE.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. BL-972's contact-shape ruling (operator ruling, batch 256) chose option 1, "dispatch, then ask". It did not choose option 2, a non-blocking ask, or option 3, accepting the hold. A non-blocking ask puts a question to the operator while the lead keeps receiving task notifications and acting on them. BL-972 records that whether the harness offers one is unmeasured, and nothing in this tree measures it. What is measured is the blocking shape of `AskUserQuestion` as graph ran it: over the 246 deduplicated asks of s317–s322, no notification-carrying record and no lead assistant record falls between a question and its answer (DLV-INSIDE 0 and ACT-INSIDE 0 on every sprint line of `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/out/ask_split.txt`). That is a reading of one tool on the harness versions graph used, not a census of the harness's routes to the operator.

There is no graph pipeline step behind this entry: it is a probe of the Claude Code harness, and `ask_split.txt` is cited as data, not as a sprint reading the entry closes on; the cost lives in the harness's `AskUserQuestion`, outside this tree, and BL-972 owns its measurement on graph.

Owed: a probe, run in a scratch Claude Code session and never against a consumer, that puts one question to the operator by each candidate route while a background lane is running, and records two things per route: whether the lane's task notification reaches the lead's model before the answer, and whether the lead can make a tool call before the answer. The result is recorded here with the Claude Code version it ran on. A route that does both is evidence for the operator to re-rule BL-972's contact shape on; it changes no rule by itself, and Rule 11(a)'s requirement to ask an ambiguity question in `AskUserQuestion` stands until then.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The subject is the harness's behaviour, which is outside this tree, and the probe needs a live interactive session with an operator answering the question, which no runner here can drive. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a `has` on a note naming a route is closable by prose and shows nothing about whether notifications arrive. What this does not establish: a probe on one Claude Code version says nothing about later versions, so a negative result is dated by the version it names.

Related: BL-972.

Provenance: batch 256, filed by the b256-N10 owner under lead ai-dlc-a7; id allocated by ai-dlc-a7.
