## BL-1016 — a handoff's "Full-collection run" can be a partial pytest invocation, and QA accepts it

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. The dev's handoff record and QA's precondition both name the run "full-collection", but only the dev side states what that means. The QA side checks that a run was recorded, not that it was the full collection.

What the two role files say, read at origin/main f6e62986e. In `core/team-roles/dev.md`, section `## QA Handoff Evidence` (near :304), the dev is told: "Run the project's canonical test command: repo root, full collection, no test-name filter, marker filter or deselect." In `core/team-roles/qa.md`, section `## Handoff Evidence Precondition` (near :76-94), QA is told to "REJECT without validating further when any of these is absent: - the dev's full-collection run: exact invocation, working directory, and collected / passed / failed / deselected / skipped / xfailed counts". The only invocation shapes that list refuses are `none, <reason>` outside a record-only diff, an unwaived `none, <AC-id> waives it`, and "a `pytest --collect-only` or `pytest --co` invocation". Nothing in the precondition refuses an invocation that names test files or node ids, or that carries `-k`, `-m` or `--deselect`. A partial run with counts is "present", and QA validates on. Core already has the grammar that would refuse it, and applies it to QA's own run only. `core/scripts/merge-review-shards.sh` (header, near :209-211, at origin/main 74f2cbcf0) refuses a `Canonical run:` whose invocation narrows the collection, by `core/scripts/record-only-diff.sh`'s RUN-FILTER: "`-k` in every spelling, `--deselect`, … a file, node-id or test-file-suffix argument to any runner, an argument after `--`, and a `-m` marker or path argument the project's `docs/coding-conventions.md` does not declare canonical". `core/team-roles/qa.md` (near :328-333 at origin/main 74f2cbcf0) holds QA's `Canonical run:` to that rule. `core/scripts/validate-handoff-evidence.sh` never calls it: `run-filter` occurs 0 times there, against 16 in `merge-review-shards.sh` (case-insensitive grep at origin/main 74f2cbcf0). QA's own canonical run is therefore already filter-checked. The gap is the dev-side record: what QA accepts as the dev's full-collection run, and what the handoff validator lets through.

The instance. In graph s322, story 1.3's Dev Agent Record at its gate-2 go-signal c6ebe5c96 reads `Full-collection run: .venv/bin/python -m pytest --tb=short -m "not integration" server/tests/test_s317_4_3b_fixture_predicate_twin.py server/tests/test_s315_4_base_binding.py server/tests/test_s322_1_3_nav_count_derivation_twin.py -q` and adds "The repo-wide suite was not run". QA's report accepts it as present: "The invocation is the lane-15a three-file run, not a repo-wide run" (graph `docs/reviews/s322/3-qa-validation.md:14`). QA then ran the server suite itself, and it was green. That is the shape of BL-397's motivating s315 story 1.5, whose dev "ran only two pytest files and never ran the full server suite" (graph `docs/reviews/s315/5-qa-report.md:29`).

The population, s317 to s322, by `docs/analysis/step-value-b256/bl397/handoff_runs.py` at graph ed61de745 (output in `docs/analysis/step-value-b256/bl397/out/handoff_runs.txt`).
- Population: every primary QA report (`docs/reviews/s<N>/<story>-qa-validation.md`, no `-p<k>` pass suffix, outside `shards/`). Each is joined to its story file, read at the handoff sha named in the report's go-signal, reviewed-sha or range line. 114 reports: 15 are not story reports (sprint-review and party fixes), and 26 story files carry no record line, all in s317 to s319, before the column-0 form.
- The record: the last `Full-collection run` line, in either the column-0 form or the earlier `- Full-collection run, cwd …` bullet. Its invocation is the first backticked span that invokes pytest.
- The classes: PARTIAL names a `.py` file or node id, or carries `-k`, `-m` or `--deselect`. COLLECT-ONLY runs no test. FULL is any other pytest invocation (a directory or none; a footprint-watchdog wrapper is judged on its arguments after ` -- `). NON-PYTEST is a vitest, jest or bash harness, or a line with no invocation, and is not judged. NONE-CLAIM is `none, …`.
- Result: 73 handoffs carry a record line. 50 are FULL, 4 are PARTIAL, 1 is COLLECT-ONLY, 13 are NON-PYTEST and 5 are NONE-CLAIM. So 5 of the 55 judged pytest records (5 of 73 record lines) are not a repo-root full collection:
  - s317 4-3b: one file, `server/test_live_smoke.py`.
  - s320 3-3: from `server/`, with `-m "not integration"`.
  - s321 5-4: from the repo root over `tests/`, with `-m "not integration"`.
  - s322 1.3: three files plus `-m`.
  - s322 1.6: `--collect-only`, under an operator waiver the story records.
- Rejects: QA rejected one of the five on the precondition, s317 4-3b. It is among the seven rejects BL-397's b255 paragraph lists, and its own words call it "a process record, not a code defect". The other four went through.
- Controls, same run: s322 story 1.8's watchdog line (`… -- -n 8 -q --tb=short`) reads FULL, and story 1.3 reads PARTIAL. Two synthetic lines also classify as expected: `pytest -m "not integration" server/tests` reads PARTIAL and `pytest rebalancer/tests -n 8` reads FULL.
- Limits: 4 NON-PYTEST rows carry no invocation at all ("see dev-report", "not executed", a status word, a sha), so the count is over parsed records. graph's own server convention, `docs/coding-conventions.md:808` at ed61de745, names the canonical run as "`pytest -n 8 server/tests` run from the repository root". The same line goes on: "with NO `-k`, NO `-m`, and NO `--deselect` … a reduced `-m` run is diagnostic-only and MUST NOT be cited as evidence". The `-m "not integration"` runs are therefore partial under the consumer's rule too, wherever they ran from: graph's conventions declare no `not integration` marker canonical, and line 808 forbids `-m`. The script reads any `-m` as PARTIAL and does not consult the conventions file, which is cruder than RUN-FILTER: graph declares one `-m` run canonical, the repo-root `-m unit` run (graph `docs/coding-conventions.md:810-813` at ed61de745). None of the 4 PARTIALs is that run. Two name files (s317 4-3b, s322 1.3), and three carry `-m "not integration"`, one from `server/` (s320 3-3), one from the repo root over `tests/` (s321 5-4). So the crude rule misclassifies none of them. RUN-FILTER itself was not executed on them.

What graph's own override requires. `.claude/skills/ai-dlc/overrides/team-roles__dev__bug-class-sweep.md` shadows dev.md's `Workflow Per Task`, and its item 15 already carried the requirement at graph e859fa701 (s315's planning anchor, :64-75). It still carries it at ed61de745 (:71-72): "**Honest-green citation (HARD GATE):** the run cited as gate evidence is the project's canonical test command — repo root, full collection, real configuration. No subset selection (`-k`, `-m`, `--deselect`, or equivalents) … Reduced runs are for iteration only and MUST NOT be cited as gate evidence." Story 1.3's cited run names three files and carries `-m`, so it breaks that HARD GATE on both counts. The override names its enforcer: "Gate validation checks #4 and #5 will reject stories missing this evidence". Nothing enforced it. QA accepted the run, and the story reached `done` in graph's s322 sprint-status. This filing did not read checks #4 and #5's text, so whether they cannot see an invocation's shape or simply did not fire is not established.

The fix, not built: the Handoff Evidence Precondition and `validate-handoff-evidence.sh` read the dev's `Full-collection run:` invocation through `core/scripts/record-only-diff.sh run-filter` with the project's `docs/coding-conventions.md`, the rule `core/scripts/merge-review-shards.sh` already holds QA's own `Canonical run:` to, and REJECT what it refuses. A conventions-declared marker run, such as graph's repo-root `-m unit` run (graph `docs/coding-conventions.md:810-813` at ed61de745), still passes.

Closes when that routing is in core, proven by a runner-wired arm that fails on today's text with three seeds:
- story 1.3's line, which must be refused;
- story 1.8's line, which must pass;
- a declared-canonical `-m` line (a repo-root `-m unit` run, with a conventions file declaring it), which must pass.

There is no graph pipeline step behind this entry: it is a correctness defect in core's QA precondition and its handoff validator, and `docs/analysis/step-value-b256/bl397/handoff_runs.py` is a census of graph handoff records, not a reading of a consumer sprint's step cost; the cost lives in core/team-roles/qa.md and core/scripts/validate-handoff-evidence.sh.

Provenance: batch 256, cluster b256-M5, from the BL-397 value measurement; id allocated by lead ai-dlc-a7.

verify: manual

## BL-999 — a verdict or pass record written outside a git hook (SIGPIPE at its default) is read by a push gate running inside one (SIGPIPE ignored), so a recorded pass can disagree with the hook's verdict

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. A push gate runs inside git's pre-push hook, where SIGPIPE is ignored, so a writer whose reader exits early gets EPIPE: it prints a `Broken pipe` diagnostic and exits non-zero instead of dying silently. Records the push skips on are also written by runs outside any hook, where SIGPIPE is at its default. A unit whose verdict depends on that difference can therefore be recorded as a pass outside the hook and be skipped by a push whose own run of it would fail.

Executed probe (a scratch repo with a bare `file://` remote, under mktemp; git 2.56.0, bash 3.2.57). The repo's pre-push hook runs a snippet and records the writer's `PIPESTATUS[0]` and its stderr; the same snippet runs again outside any hook, from the same script:
- `yes | head -1`: inside the hook `yes` exits 1, outside it exits 141 (killed by SIGPIPE). Neither printed anything on stderr: BSD `yes` exits on the write error without a message.
- `grep -h . <300000-line file> | head -1`: inside the hook grep exits 2 and prints `grep: stdout: Broken pipe`, outside it exits 141 with empty stderr.
- The push exited 0 and the remote ref was created, so the hook ran.

Instance 1, the verdict seeder. `scripts/seed-verdict-store.sh` runs each named fixture through `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh` (its header: "Each named fixture runs serially through core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh with the invocation counter armed") from an ordinary shell, and its passes land in the counted store a push reads. Neither file installs a PIPE disposition: `seed-verdict-store.sh` carries no `trap` token at all, and `hermetic-run.sh`'s one trap line is `trap '[ "${AI_DLC_HERMETIC_KEEP:-0}" = 1 ] || rm -rf "$HR_WORK"' EXIT` (`:149`), with 0 lines naming PIPE in either file. Control: the same count finds 6 `trap` lines in `.githooks/pre-push`, also 0 naming PIPE, so the hook's ignored SIGPIPE comes from git, as the probe shows, and not from the hook. The seeder was not run for this entry. The case it bit: 0.1081.0's fix of `i75_failsclosed` in `scripts/validate-enforcement-map.sh` (now `:6548`, `i75_norm "$1" | awk '/exit [0-9]|:-\/nonexistent/ { f = 1 } END { exit (f ? 0 : 1) }'`). At base the reader was `i75_norm | grep -qE`, and its writers printed `Broken pipe` only under the hook's ignored SIGPIPE, which turned `validator-arm-selection`'s stderr compare red on a push (BL-868). The comment above `i75_failsclosed` on origin/main records the base measurement with SIGPIPE ignored: "two batches of 40 runs: the sed line 40 and 40 times, the grep line 34 and 23 times". The 0.1081.0 CHANGELOG entry gives the figure as sed 40 / grep 24 Broken pipe at base, and 0 at the fix. With SIGPIPE at its default, 0.

Instance 2, hand-run pre-suite prefixes. Owners run the hook's pre-suite prefix by hand before a push, outside any hook, and that run writes pass records into `<git common dir>/ai-dlc-presuite-keys` (`.githooks/pre-push:202`, `PK_DIR="$gcd/ai-dlc-presuite-keys"`, with `gcd` from `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`), which every worktree's push reads. A record's name ends in the first 16 hex of the present key, which `pk_present` (`:244`) hashes from the walk `/usr/bin/find . -path ./.git -prune -o -print0` (`:248`): every path with its kind and symlink target, with file content excluded (`:231`, "every path under the work tree when the push began, hashed with its kind and symlink target"). Evidence, from the BL-914 population (104 distinct on-disk logs): in 11 sessions a push's `N of 23 step(s) skipped on a recorded pass` with N>=1 followed a hand prefix in the same session minutes earlier, and in session 83ed1313 the push from `wt-F14` skipped 4 steps that a hand prefix in another worktree, `f14pre.Wnu6WP`, had recorded. The hook prints no present key, so which run wrote a given record is not attributable from the logs.

Fix shape: the seeder, the hand prefix and the hook run their units under one signal disposition. No fix in this release.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The fix decides what test exists (a disposition set in the seeder and the prefix, or a check that refuses a record written under the other one), and a receipt written only to file this entry is what BL-915 forbids.

Provenance: batch 256, M6; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual

## BL-1010 — a branch-delete push runs the full pre-push hook, about 2.5 minutes, when made without `--no-verify`

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. A `git push origin --delete <branch>` delivers no tree, yet git runs the pre-push hook for it, and the hook runs its pre-suite steps and reaches the fixture suite's whole-suite key before it exits. Neither hook looks at what is being pushed: `.githooks/pre-push:31` begins `cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" || exit 1` and the hook never reads the ref lines git hands it on stdin, so a delete (local sha all zeros) is judged on the working tree like any release push. Searched for any all-zero-sha test: `0{40}`, the literal 40-zero string and `zero=` read 0 in `.githooks/pre-push` and 0 in `core/git-hooks/pre-push`; control, the same search reads 1 in git's own `pre-push.sample` in the main checkout's `.git/hooks/`.

Evidence, batch 256 M6, from the recorded population in `docs/analysis/push-gate-cost-and-pole-value-b256.md` (section "Branch-delete pushes run the whole hook"): 7 delete pushes made without `--no-verify`, each with a `.git/ai-dlc-suite-key.log` HIT line inside its window. Start to HIT, a lower bound on the hook's wall, for the 6 whose start is on record (starts from m6-583a's transcript census): e1d32849 2026-11-10T14:35:00Z 144 s; 94d65a60 2026-11-11T15:24:18Z 157 s; b655f7b8 15:33:12Z 155 s; a9cff818 16:23:33Z 148 s; 490b0ab0 18:53:56Z 200 s; 949b78fd 19:28:31Z 258 s. The seventh, bf3ed410, is in m6-914v's table, and its log (perishable, in that session's scratchpad at `rdel.lVnW/push.out`) ends `- [deleted] b254-F975` at mtime 2026-11-11T18:29:36Z, 3 s after its HIT at 18:29:33Z; its start is not in that session's transcript, so its wall is not measured. m6-914v's table reads all 7 as green, `FIXTURE SUITE SKIPPED`, pre-suite `17 of 23` skipped, so the other 6 pre-suite steps ran on every delete.

Owed: a delete costs no hook time. Either the hook exits 0 when every ref line it is handed has an all-zero local sha, or the step that deletes a branch passes `--no-verify`. The first keeps every caller covered; the hook's own stdin is the only input that distinguishes a delete. No fix in this release.

There is no graph pipeline step; the cost lives in this repo's pre-push hook. It is not filed as an efficiency entry, because the tag would bring E3b's Baseline/Step/Movement filing arms, and the lead ruled it untagged. Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The fix decides the test: a delete fed to the real hook exiting before its first step, with a release push as the control. A receipt written only to file this entry is what BL-915 forbids.

Provenance: batch 256, M6, from m6-583a's push census; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual

## BL-1013 — fixture-hygiene `--matrix` fans out every mutant's drives unbounded, about 120 concurrent validator runs, past the box's process-group line, while a bounded width gives the same verdicts

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. In `core/fixtures/fixture-hygiene/run.sh` the matrix block (`if [ "$MODE" = matrix ]; then`, `:285`) loops over 20 mutants (`for m in MH1 MH2 ... MM4B MH9; do`, `:287`). For each one it starts six background drives: `drive "$m.d"`, `drive "$m.v" ... --derive` and `drive "$m.c"` (`:290`), and `d7drive "$m.7"`, `d8drive "$m.8"` and `d9drive "$m.9"` (`:291`). It then waits once, after the loop (`done; wait`, `:292`). That is 20 x 6 = 120 drives in flight at once, each a full validator run, with nothing bounding the width. The run-rules line of 150 members per process group is crossed by construction. The plain run (`:306-311`) starts 26 concurrent drives the same way (3 at `:306`, 14 at `:307`, 2 at `:308`, 2 at `:309` and 5 at `:310`) before its one `wait` at `:311`.

By construction, the width should not move a verdict. `drive()` (`:235-238`) writes `$WORK/c.<tag>.out`, `.err` and `.rc` per tag, and the matrix reads them only after the `wait`. One caveat: the `$m.d` and `$m.v` drives run in the same world directory, `mut-$m`, at the same time. Measured at width 6, once, by m6-h9 at the release tip. The run used a scratch copy whose only change is an added `wait` after each mutant's six backgrounded drives, so 20 rounds of 6. It was started as `bash core/fixtures/fixture-hygiene/run.sh --matrix` from the worktree root, because `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh` passes the fixture no arguments. Result: rc 0, empty stderr, and a peak process group of 36 members (a watchdog at 100 never fired). It printed 20 `MATRIX` lines, and each equals its committed `# MUTANT-MATRIX` row once `twins=` is normalised to `twins=-`, the DW and MH9 rows included. The fixture-hygiene `# MUTANT-MATRIX` rows were measured through that width-6 scratch edit only, never by a plain matrix run, and the unbounded width was not run.

Owed: the matrix and plain runs bound their concurrency to a fixed width, so a `--matrix` run stays inside one process-group budget. No fix in this release.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The fix decides the test, and a receipt written only to file this entry is what BL-915 forbids.

Provenance: batch 256, M6; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual

## BL-1017 — step 7 of `/ai-dlc-update` relays no `NOTE` row except `reconcile-log-name`, so any other `NOTE` that `apply.sh` prints can go unrelayed to the operator

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. Step 7 of `core/skills/ai-dlc-update/SKILL.md` names three row kinds the session acts on: `RESOLVED` (`:1888`, "already done, no operator step"), `WORKLIST` (`:1895`, "the only things left for YOU. Work EVERY row") and `DECISION` (`:1910`, "a genuine operator call"). It then says "Do NOT re-do a `RESOLVED` row by hand. Work only the `WORKLIST` and `DECISION` rows" (`:1925`). Its only instruction for a `NOTE` row is the paragraph at `:1923`, which covers one kind, `NOTE reconcile-log-name` (write the reconcile log at that path); none of the other 13 kinds has one, so nothing tells the session to read them, relay them or ask about them. graph's installed 0.1061.0 `SKILL.md:1843` carries the same wording (read by the lead; not re-read here). Any `NOTE` of those 13 kinds that `apply.sh` prints can therefore go unrelayed.

Population, re-derived at the tip of the BL-978 unit with `grep -n '^[^#]*say NOTE' core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/apply.sh`: 16 sites over 14 kinds, against a control of 46 `say WORKLIST` sites from the same pattern. origin/main carries 16 sites over 14 kinds; BL-978 adds none (its `scaffold-ignored` and `scaffold-ignore-unchecked` rows are WORKLIST at every tier). The count is a floor: a kind passed through a variable is invisible to the grep.

Each site classified by READING its text, answering whether an operator wants it relayed:
- 334 `report-verified`: no, it confirms the union-gate condition passed.
- 463 `blockers-resolved`: no, the approval already covers the region.
- 465 `blockers-resolved`: no, the unseen rows were already re-approved on record.
- 483 `report-unverified`: yes, nothing confirmed an operator approved a region matching theirs.
- 1271 `finish-template-unrecorded`: yes, the text asks for a by-hand confirmation of each template-derived file.
- 1274 `finish-template-manifest-unreadable`: yes, a merge was not re-tested and the text names the restore.
- 2155 `override-adjudicated`: no, a recorded verdict stands and nothing is prescribed.
- 2233 `override-readopt-subsumed`: yes, it says to skip the readopt that an earlier row prints.
- 2334 `extension-adjudicated`: no, "no re-read is prescribed".
- 2338 `extension-retire`: yes, the recorded verdict authorizes deleting the entry.
- 2340 `extension-contradicts-core`: yes, it asks for a refile.
- 2342 `extension-adjudicated-unrouted`: yes, the register entry has to be checked against the verdict enum.
- 2983 `reconcile-log-name`: covered, `SKILL.md:1923` instructs it.
- 3170 `finish-unverified` (empty setup-sited set): yes, no changed setup-sited path could be verified.
- 3203 `finish-unverified` (setup-sited bucket): yes, the text asks for a by-hand confirmation.
- 3246 `finish-classify-unrecorded`: yes, each `semantic-merge` row needs a by-hand check.

That is 10 sites an operator wants relayed and has no instruction for, 5 that need no relay, and 1 already covered.

Owed: a step-7 instruction for the other `NOTE` kinds (which to relay and which to leave), or a move of the 10 relay-worthy sites to a kind step 7 already works. Which of the two is a design call for the builder.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The owed work is a judgment of which kinds an operator wants, and a tree predicate would read CLOSE-CANDIDATE on any edit to step 7's prose.

Provenance: batch 256, filed by the BL-978 owner at the lead's ruling; id allocated by the lead.

## BL-1021 — `FORK_BUDGET` carries 136 forks of headroom, so the fork ceiling cannot see a rise smaller than that

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. The fork ceiling in `fork-budget.txt` is `FORK_BUDGET=2926` (line 69 at origin/main, the one line the reader's `^FORK_BUDGET=<digits>$` anchor accepts). The file's own convention is stated at lines 49, 54, 60 and 68: "The budget is the tip reading plus HEADROOM_MARGIN 2." The tip reading is 2790, so the convention gives 2792, and the committed budget sits 136 above it (2926 - 2790 = 136).

Measured by the BL-978 builder, each reading `bash scripts/fork-profile.sh --section total` in a clean detached worktree, exit 0:
- origin/main `74f2cbcf0`: TOTAL 2790.
- origin/main `abc25f555`: TOTAL 2790.

So a change that adds fewer than 136 forks passes the ceiling unseen, and the ratchet that is meant to catch a rise of a few forks (the margin is 2) cannot fire on one. A ceiling that nothing can reach reads exactly like one that passed.

Owed: one ratchet of `FORK_BUDGET` to the tip reading plus `HEADROOM_MARGIN` 2, in a release built after the batch queue drains. It is NOT done in the BL-978 release, because lowering the budget now would collide with every queued release that raises it. BL-978's +4 raise (to 2930) stays as measured.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The owed work is a re-measurement at the tip after the queue drains, and no predicate over the tree today can state the future tip reading.

Provenance: batch 256, filed by the BL-978 owner at the lead's ruling, from the final delta adversary's NOTE; id allocated by the lead (ai-dlc-a7).

## BL-1007 — escalation-status-vocabulary is green only from the repo root: run from `/`, its 15 mutant and refusal lines FAIL, the same 15 before and after the in-process conversion

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. `core/fixtures/escalation-status-vocabulary/run.sh` passes from the repository root and fails from `/`. The pre-push hook runs every fixture from the root, so the push sees only the green run.

Measured on one plain run per side, each output saved and read back:
- From `/`, at 3e0800c07 (before the conversion): 42 ok, 15 FAIL.
- From `/`, with this release's converted fixture (before its `MUTANT-MATRIX` block was added): 48 ok, 15 FAIL.
- From the root, the same converted fixture: 63 ok, 0 FAIL, PASS.

The two sorted FAIL sets from `/` are byte-identical, 15 lines each, so the cwd dependence predates the conversion. Control: the ok line `(a) an appended resolution status is the token adjudicated` is present once in both `/` runs and absent from both FAIL sets, and the two `/` runs share 42 ok lines.

The 15 lines are the battery: three unmutated controls and the twelve mutants scored against them. From `/`, every control fails itself:
- the whole-directory control scores 00000 against a wanted 11111;
- the refusal control scores 000000 against 111111;
- the reader control answers offender=2 and near-miss=2.

Every call answers rc 2 because the copies cannot resolve a project root, so each mutant scored against those controls fails too.

From the root, every control holds (11111, 111111, offender reported and near-miss quiet). Each mutant also scores its own distinct vector, for example M-revert 00111, R-rc 011011 and R-revert-all 000011.

So green-from-root does NOT mean those arms assert nothing at the root. Their controls' positive conjuncts hold there, and every mutant is told apart from its control. It means the fixture needs a project root to resolve its copies. This is not the `core/fixtures/check-3b-locked-anchor` hazard (green because the cwd lacks the decoys): from the wrong cwd this fixture goes red, not green.

What is wrong is that nothing states the dependence. A fixture that must hold from any cwd asserts cwd-invariance in its own arms; this one neither asserts it nor refuses a non-root cwd with FIXTURE BROKEN, so it reports 15 assertion failures that are not regressions.

Not measured here: the `/` run of the tip as it now stands, after its `MUTANT-MATRIX` block changed how the mutants are scored per push.

Not fixed here.

Provenance: batch 256, cluster N11; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual

## BL-1006 — `gate-resume`'s self-probe verdict depends on load

**DEFECT** (BL-868 class). OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. `core/fixtures/gate-resume/run.sh:118` runs `bash "$CKPT" --self-probe`, and a non-zero exit is the fixture's `bad "gate-checkpoint.sh --self-probe FAILED ..."`. That self-probe's Direction 10 ("concurrent writers lose nothing") starts twenty `record` writers in parallel in a `while [ "$_i" -le 20 ]` loop, one backgrounded `bash "$_me" ... record "c${_i}" PASS` each (`core/scripts/gate-checkpoint.sh:192-199`). Each writer takes a mkdir lock with the loop `until mkdir "$_lock"`, which counts `_tries` and, at `[ "$_tries" -ge 200 ]`, prints `gate-checkpoint: FAIL -- could not take ${_lock} after ${_tries} tries.` and runs `exit 2`, otherwise `sleep 0.05` (`core/scripts/gate-checkpoint.sh:354-364`).

How established: a static read of the code during BL-915's (c) audit. No load run was made.

Under contention, a writer can exhaust the bounded retry (200 tries of `sleep 0.05`, in effect a wall-clock bound of about ten seconds of sleeping per writer) and exit 2 independent of the correctness of the checkpoint ledger. Direction 10 then finds fewer than 20 rows and the probe exits 2, which `gate-resume` reports as a failure of the checkpoint assertions.

Owed, and not in this entry's release: fix the test side by seaming the retry bound or forcing the interleaving, never a retry, a widened bound or fewer jobs (BL-868).

Provenance: batch b256 BL-915 (c) audit; lead ai-dlc-a7.

verify: manual

## BL-1014 — a full `--matrix` re-measure of adversarial-shard-merge costs about 4.8 to 8.3 hours in one invocation and 6.8 to 10.3 hours in 11 chunks, so the per-start cost and the starts per cell are the lever

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. For `core/fixtures/adversarial-shard-merge`, a full `--matrix` re-measure is 87 mutants × 90 arms and costs about 4.8 to 8.3 hours in one invocation (a 701 s base phase plus 87 passes of 192 to 336 s, five passes measured), about 6.8 to 10.3 hours when run in 11 chunks. That figure is DERIVED; the full run was not timed.

Operator ruling: that burden is too much for one change, so no full or scoped matrix run is a per-change duty (`BL-1022` amends `.claude/rules/fixture-mutants.md` clause 2 to say so).

Starts: the matrix passes armed no invocation counter, so no starts figure was measured in the matrix. The starts figure comes from the counted adversarial-shard-merge runs: 3101 starts at the release tip, two identical per-start logs.

The lever is the per-start cost (about 0.32 s per start) and the number of starts per cell; the cell count itself is the matrix's coverage and is not the lever. Only adversarial-shard-merge's `--matrix` takes a mutant list (`--mutants=`); the other 78 of the 79 matrix-capable fixtures run it whole, so for them the burden is unchanged and this lever is what applies.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The legend allows it for an OPEN entry, and no mechanical predicate exists yet: the closing receipt is a measured full `--matrix` wall clock and process-start count, taken on one tree with wall and CPU stated, that is short enough to be an operator's routine instrument, or a lower per-start cost with the same two figures beside the old ones. A receipt that greps the fixture for an option name would be satisfied by a flag that changes no start count.

Provenance: batch 256, b256-B2 (BL-911 narrowing); id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual

## BL-1022 — fixture-mutants.md clause 2 required the matrix re-measure on every arm or mutant change, which costs about 4.8 to 8.3 hours in one invocation and 6.8 to 10.3 hours in 11 chunks for adversarial-shard-merge

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. Clause 2 of `.claude/rules/fixture-mutants.md` ("One run per distinct state") said the matrix's re-measuring command is re-run whenever an arm or a mutant is added or changed. For `core/fixtures/adversarial-shard-merge` a full re-measure is 87 mutants × 90 arms, about 4.8 to 8.3 hours in one invocation (a 701 s base phase plus 87 passes of 192 to 336 s, five passes measured), about 6.8 to 10.3 hours when run in 11 chunks, DERIVED (BL-1014 carries the per-start lever).

Operator ruling: the full re-measure is too much burden for one change, so the clause is amended for every fixture: when arms or mutants change, the required evidence is the fixture's counted run showing each declared mutant killed by its deciding arm with its twin holding, by name; Where a fixture's counted run does not itself score each mutant's deciding arm and twin, the change re-measures only the changed mutants' rows by running the fixture's `--matrix` narrowed to those mutants; a fixture whose `--matrix` takes no mutant list runs it whole. Apart from the case in the previous sentence, a full or partial `--matrix` is an operator instrument and never a per-change duty; the MUTANT-MATRIX header keeps its REMEASURE line naming the command. The heading "One run per distinct state" is unchanged, because `scripts/validate-fixture-invocations.sh` and `scripts/validate-fixture-inprocess.sh` quote it in remedy strings.

For the 78 of the 79 matrix-capable fixtures whose `--matrix` takes no mutant list (only adversarial-shard-merge's takes `--mutants=`), the burden is unchanged; that remainder is what BL-1014's lever, the per-start cost and the starts per cell, applies to.

Still owed: 79 fixture `run.sh` headers still say the matrix is re-measured whenever an arm or a mutant changes (measured: `git grep -l -i -E 're-measured whenever an arm or (a )?(the )?mutant' <rev> -- 'core/fixtures/*/run.sh'` returns 79, and an impossible-phrase control returns 0). Reconciling them with the amended clause 2 is left OPEN, because they are measured fixture files.

Rides batch 256's release, in the same change as BL-911.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The legend gives no runner-wired form for a rule edit; the closing receipt is `scripts/validate-claude-rules.sh` and the enforcement map green on the amended file, plus the heading present verbatim in `.claude/rules/fixture-mutants.md`. A receipt that greps the file for a word of the new clause would be satisfied by a comment.

Provenance: batch 256, b256-B2; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual
